## Introduction

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/1/)

This **in-progress guide** is intended to help players to get a handle of Monster Hunter Rise, primarily from a single-player perspective. All readers are welcome to share feedback based on how it is going whether through PMs or any other way that works, though you can also see the basic plan on the Version Changes page.

Some information may differ from the demo or information you heard in pre-release content like reviews (largely based on Version 1.0.0), but you can also expect this guide to be outdated at some point as well. I'll address this if possible, but it is presently based on Version 3.0.0 of the Nintendo Switch European release. I will try to account for the Japanese version (which has more languages) and the upcoming PC release in 2022 where possible.

#### **Frequently Asked Questions (FAQ)**

- **Where can I train and learn how to use my weapons?**
  - Against the Toadversary and more in the **Training Area**. To get there you have to go through the **Buddy Plaza** and take a ferry ride there to the Training Area, or you can alternatively use fast travel if you **hold the Minus Button** (![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/113/)) .
  - You do not use any items while practicing in this area and can set the Wirebug Gauge to unlimited.
- **How does changing my character (gender, race, etc.) and Character Edit Vouchers work?**
  - The vouchers allow you to access the Character Creation menu for your hunter to change their appearance completely, but **you cannot change your name**.
  - The first voucher is free to use, but there is a cost to purchase additional edits in real money ( **not** Kamura Points or Zenny).
    - Writer's note: I highly recommend that you focus on fashion hunting with layered armour. Your voice can be changed with the language options as well, so you may enjoy one of the other two voice overs.
    - On a related note, changing your Cahoot's appearance is completely free while you can choose presets for your Palico and Palamute for Iori to scout. They can be renamed at no cost.
- **Can you pet the dog, cat and bird?**
  - Yep. Just tell them to **Wait!** using the Action Bar (![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/108/)to scroll, ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/112/)to select) and approach them for options. You can choose from **Give Treat, Shake or Pet** for your Palamutes. Palicos have **Play, High Five and Pet**. The Cohoot needs to be called with the **Call Cahoot** Action Bar option in select locations, but you can walk around if it perched on your arm or send it to a few places around the village.
- **Can I rename my buddies - the Palicoes, Palamutes and Cohoot?**
  - Yes. Go to the **Buddy Plaza** and talk to **Iori, the Buddy Handler** then choose the **Rename Buddy** option.
  - You can only interact with the Cohort in single-player, but can call it with the Action Bar's **Call Cohoot** option which even allows you to change its look as well as the name.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/6/)

- **Why am I automatically crafting things?**
  - The game defaults to assuming you want to craft select items when you have the required materials, these settings can be adjusted in your Crafting List and may have some benefits during a hunt to manage storage and prepare the tools you need on the job.
- **Why should I slay a monster instead of capturing it with traps and tranqs during a hunt?**
  - Capturing a monster may be faster at the cost of preparation, but it also loses out on unique drops from monsters that can be seen if you look in related monster's "Materials". You access this by pausing going to Hunter's Notes -> Large Monsters -> Materials.
  - Some rewards can come from multiple actions like capturing, broken parts and carves but you should mix it up and check what you need to do.
- **What language do the characters speak in?**
  - In-universe, it is the fictional Wyverian language or "Monster Hunter Language" but the game is only written in our languages with the options depending on your region of the game (Korean, Simplified Chinese and Traditional Chinese aren't in the western release so far). The voices include Monster Hunter Language as well as English or Japanese which can be changed in your menu.

## Quests

> This page covers all quests (minus Arena, Event and Rampage) within the HR20 boundary as of Ver 2.0.0. This means **all Village and Hub Quests (1-6★), but also many 7★ Hub Quests**. There are missing quests which are locked by HR progression in the Hub, but also a chance that some more quests may become avaliable for earlier parts through patches, so please consider finishing as many different quests as possible if something seems to be missing.
>
> I am very aware that there is randomness at play for High Rank quests that may make the details more vague than earlier (since non-targets leave or get replaced - besides unstable environments in the **5★** Hub Quests), but the information is there as a placeholder till I do some further research. It seems safe to say that it is based on a pool of monsters associated with a location, but also matches the **★** while obviously not pulling too many monsters from updates.
>
> Quest numbers were removed from an earlier draft but will return when they can be implemented better along with links. You can check the [Version Changes](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/switch/300976-monster-hunter-rise/faqs/79167/version-changes) page for a better sense of where the guide is going.
>
> **Please provide feedback if anything seems strange, superb or suboptimal.**

* * *

| **Back to Basics** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1** ★ | Locale: Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: 120 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Smaller monsters include: Izuchi, Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Felyne, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this TRAINING quest is to **complete each of Master Utsushi's tasks**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This quest is fairly straightforward since Master Utsushi just guides you through the motions of learning to use your weapon against a pack of Izuchi, working with Buddies and Wirebug movement action out in the open much like in the demo.

You will only be encouraged to do this while being introduced to the **Tent** within the **Base Camp, Supply Box** and using **Fast Travel** to return to **Camp** but you have almost a whole hour in real-time to explore. This is a safe opportunity to fill in the map and discover **Endemic Life** to review in your Hunter's Notes, even if Master Utsushi won't let his ace student hear the end of it.

**Remember that even if the time limit seems generous, it is best to use it to ensure the greatest success in victory.**

* * *

| **Learning to Ride** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1** ★ | Locale: Arena | Locale Info: No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: 120 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Smaller monsters include: (None) |
| Your goal in this TRAINING quest is to **learn to Wyvern Ride from Master Utsushi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This should be your first time in the Arena (the setting of Arena Quests), but this time is just a practice run to get familiar with riding Wyverns with Ironsilk. Whether you want to bond with monsters to take other monsters like the Monster Riders from Monster Hunter Stories or prefer to bon- bang their heads into walls like Monster Hunter World, you can do a lot of damage potentially with both in a single ride if you are up to it.

First, you need to get an Arzuros into a **Mountable State**, which can be done faster with Silkbind attacks and Wirebug jumping attacks which both do " **special damage**" for mounting (marked by a blue background and yellow text) or by using a **Puppet Spider**. This quest is special as the normal timer for riding a monster is infinite, so you may want to return to practice movement and using Mounted Punishers.

On that note, when the second Arzuros arrives you will have the opportunity to try out the special move by pressing both the Normal Attack and Special Attack buttons at the same time when the Wyvern Riding Gauge is built up but mind that you have a time limit before you are bucked off besides other risks. The gauge being full also makes the pair of you able to topple monsters with ease which makes it easier to land the coup de grace though this game will autocorrect you if you are a decent distance from your target when you press the input.

* * *

| **The Basics of Capturing Monsters** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1** ★ | Locale: Arena | Locale Info: No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: 120 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Your goal in this TRAINING quest is to **learn how to capture from Master Utsushi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Normally when "capturing a monster" is talked about in games, it refers to throwing some shape to catch a weakened beast or taking an egg to raise a newborn monster as your kin. Here and now as a Monster Hunter, the reason to catch a monster to get materials that you would normally miss out on if you only carved a slain monster.

It is more complicated to do, but faster if executed properly. The steps are still simple in principle. First, weaken the monster by thrashing it. Second, use a trap to restraint the monster's movements. Third and finally, hit the monster with enough Tranq Bombs or Tranq Ammo till it passes out. Mind that you void the opportunity to carve when you do this, even if you do get rewards.

You can't complete this task till you grab all of the items you need from the Supply Box, so be sure to collect an EZ Pitfall Trap, EZ Shock Trap and EZ Tranq Bomb that you'll put to use against yet another Arzuros. The Blue Icon on the Target Icon means a capturable monster and be sure not to slay it.

- Yellow: notices the hunter and is on guard.
- Red: actively aggressive towards the hunter.
- Purple: on the move but still aggressive.
- Blue: damaged to a certain threshold and open to being captured.

You will also be introduced to Large Monster Statuses which can vary much like your own. Just keep an eye out for a blue "eye-con" in this case but many others exist with them being displayed on the map as well as under the targeting system in the upper right corner of your screen. A couple of tranq bombs on a trapped monster will get the job down.

* * *

| **Roly-poly Lanterns** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1** ★ | Locale: Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: 240 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 8 Firelanterns**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Your first single-star quest as a hunter of Kamura but it is too soon to go out for marshmallows. This delivery-style quest is an introduction to collecting and returning items as part of a quest. There are two kinds of item to consider; the first is **Account Items** that are automatically delivered as soon as they are gathered (nice and easy) and the second are **Transport Items** which you need to transport to the blue Supply Box in person as they are too bulky to fit in your Item Pouch.

Fortunately, Firelanterns are all smaller account items so the quest will be complete as long as you salvage with a moderate amount of speed, though you should keep in mind that delivered items cannot be taken back. Whether you focus on collecting in the south near your Base Camp and Area 1 or decide to venture into the north of the Shrine Ruins for resources it is up to you. It is probably best to get exposed to Endemic Life, smaller monsters and more while it is still safe.

* * *

| **Fungal Frustrations** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale: Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: 240 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 8 Unique Mushrooms**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

In this case, you'll find the Unique Mushrooms among Mushroom Colonies on higher ground to the north but it is clustered fairly close together and they are all account items that are immediately sent back to Camp.

* * *

| **Unpious Peons** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1** ★ | Locale: Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: 240 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Smaller monsters include: Jagras ( **Target**), Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Felyne, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay 8 Jagras**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

These smaller monsters are usually found in packs and move as a unit whether deciding to attack or flee, this makes it easier to clear the quota if you follow the red icons on your map.

* * *

| **Help with Maintenance** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1** ★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>240 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Gajau, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Izuchi ( **Target**), Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay 8 Izuchi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Known for their sharp tails and even sharper teamwork, the Izuchi tend to hunt in packs with two of their best being with a leader at almost all times though there have been no sightings. They could be part of a unit of scouts due to report back, but makes it for an easier objective with no large wild monsters about either way.

* * *

| **Great Izuchi, Great Pain** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2** ★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>900 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Great Izuchi ( **Target**) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Izuchi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The Great Izuchi can be difficult to separate from its first and second in command, but as long as you stay resourceful, you may be able to lead it towards the Bombadgy can be used as a way to land some big damage if you hit them towards the Izuchi. Otherwise, it is your hunting team against the Izuchi. You don't need to worry about a second large monster getting in the way, but you can't use them for a Mounted Punisher either.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/22/)

* * *

| **Grizzly Glutton** |
| --- |
| Rank: 2★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1200 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Arzuros **(Target)**<br>Great Izuchi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Arzuros**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Your Supply Box includes some handy resources that can either help you get out of a pinch if you end up in too much trouble particularly with the Great Izuchi roaming around with its groupies with the EZ Flash Bomb. This hunt can actually be made easier if you use the riding option to control the Izuchi and you can take advantage of a brief turf war for an opening as well.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/40/)

Arzuros is known for its gluttony to the point of its illustration depicting the habit, so you should expect that it will grab you to snatch your supply if need be. This can be escaped to get an opening with Wirefall, though it may also track down honeycombs (see Area 13) if it gets the chance. It is easy to fall into the trap of finding the beast predictable, so you should be wary of how it can angle its running attack, throw rumble forward and has a butt stomp for when foes linger behind.

* * *

| **Plump and Juicy** |
| --- |
| Rank: 2★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>810 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Great Izuchi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 10 Raftshells**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

You don't really have any special supplies to work with besides the usual First-Aid and EZ Rations, but the only wild monster roaming the islands this time is the Great Izuchi. That leaves a lot of room to explore the Frost Islands while getting used to sliding more easily from a run near slopes besides getting familiar with some of the smaller monsters like the Zamite. The actual gathering part is simple as Oyster Beds are indicated on your map and the shells are delivered back automatically as Account Items instantly.

* * *

| **A Frosty Paradise** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>810 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi **(Target)**, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay 8 Baggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you don't want to end up out cold, you should be wary of the sleep-inducing fluid that these smaller monsters can use though it is fortunately only done as part of a blockable projectile attack and you can also use the Energy Drinks in the Supply Box to counteract the ailment. You should also mind that they attack with their whole weight rather than focusing on tail strikes.

* * *

| **Out Cold** |
| --- |
| Rank: 2★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>900 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Great Baggi **(Target)**<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Baggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The alpha of the Baggis is much like its smaller comrades with greater mobility and control to strike with any part of its body, however, the cunning creature knows that its size can be a risk. This is clear in how it regularly leaps back to get a good angle for the tranquilising projectile which also gives room for its allies to make their attempts. They work as a team, but far more independently than the Izuchi so you may have to be wary to avoid being swarmed in a Baggi's Paradise. Take advantage of Lagombi clashing with your target and be sure to take an Energy Drink for a better chance to strike the king's crest.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/42/)

* * *

| **Rabid Rabbit** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1200 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Lagombi (Target)**<br>Great Baggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Lagombi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Neither being hare nor bear, the raving rabbit surfs and slides across the silver snow at rapid speeds though it is less able to turn during these attacks, even if it can also reposition and retreat just as quickly. It has very sensitive hearing which enables it to sense threats but also makes the EZ Screamer Pod from the Supply Box especially effective as it attempts to get around, though they become ineffective as it becomes enraged.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/43/)

On that note, there are two types of attacking slide with one being a linear lunge that starts with the beast hunching over and a circular slide that follows it hunching in a slight coil in its posture. The defensive slides do some damage, but cannot launch you into the air so it is mainly important to recognise that a slide could be followed by a small lunge.

Other attacks to consider include its habit of throwing snowballs like the high snowball which can catch you out of the air which leaves you in Iceblight (which slows down the Wirebug gauge recovery - recover by rolling or using a Nulberry) and leaves a pile of snow that can slow down your movement. Other snowball attacks include a large snowball that only goes in front and throwing two balls to the sides, you can take the first one as a sign to reposition.

When the monster stands on its hind legs, it is a tell that it may transition to a series of actions like claw swipes but also has an attack to account for threats behind which follows it wriggling its rear. One last attack to consider is its jumping attack where the landing impact will make you lose balance even if it misses, it can be read from it hunching over and wriggling its whole body while facing a target but mind that the roll that it does after it lands is an attack as well. The target monster may leave to feed near Area 5 and go to sleep in Area 6, but if you get the aid of the Great Baggi's Mounted Punisher you may be able to force it to sleep early.

* * *

| **Case of the Creepy Crawlies** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>840 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Great Izuchi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Gargwa, **Bnahabra (Target)**, **Altaroth (Target)**, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 14 Altaroth or Bnahabra**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Your supply box includes the Poison Smoke Bomb, a popular household bug repellent bomb that may help on this extermination quest but an especially cunning hunter may even use wyvern riding to make the Great Izuchi a puppet in a plan to clear out the area of the creepy crawlies. Bnahabra can be difficult to reach so it may be worth using Kunai if they are buzzing about while the Altaroth are best defeated after they have collecting materials from the field for a greater bonus while gathering.

* * *

| **Hmm, Not So Tasty...** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale: Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>810 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo **(Target)**, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 3 Popo Tongues**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While this is a gathering quest, you still need to hunt smaller monsters before you can gather what you need and Popos are known for running away from danger whether that's a large herbivore monster, yourself after you pose a threat or both. The items will be delivered instantly, but it is best to isolate focus on the weaker Popo that are in the open or stun them to prevent a retreat if you have the option then you'll get more carving chances.

* * *

| **Feathered Frenzy** |
| --- |
| Rank: **3★** | Locale:<br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1800 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Aknosom **(Target)**<br>Arzuros | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Aknosom**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The feathered menace dances in the moonlight on a single leg which is both a show of strength and stylistic flourish hinting to what attack will come next. If you watch the umbrella-esque crest on its head, you may be able to tell apart its attacks better but there are two key things to be aware of. First is that it has a proper roar that can knock away threats in the air or stop your movement unless you use super armour or a skill to counteract this. The second is that the monster's tail feathers are quite tough so a blunt blade from the back might get punished as you bounce back.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/41/)

From close range, it is capable of performing two wide sweeps with its wings, a trio of pecks into the earth which can make it harder to punish due to the wind from the impact blowing you away from the crest and even a charging crest attack where it dashes wildly to strike anyone in its way but also can circle around. This may convince you to consider fighting from a distance, but that has its own risks as it can shoot fireballs through its flammable venom which can bounce across the land and control space. There is even a fireworks-esque spectacle that it can create both from the ground and after rising slightly into the air, so it is more ideal to draw it to fight you where there is water especially as it even makes it easier to clear any Fireblight not just stopping spreading flames.

Striking the monster from behind or an angle while being on the move is a safe bet but you do have to keep some attacks in mind like a backward kick, a backstep kick that sweeps up rumble and a hop that leads to a flash kick. It is also notable that the tip of its tail is especially tough, so you may just bounce off and get punished if you are using the wrong blademaster attack in the wrong place at the wrong time.

As you progress, you may find that it often flies away after being thrashed too much but you can stop it from retreating by using blinding tactics (Flashflies for example). If you notice that it is in Area 8 after a beatdown, rush there to potentially punish it during a meal. You also have notable openings to strike following its flipping flash kick, during its triple fireball attack and after its running charge especially if it loses balance.

* * *

| **Can't Stomach the Thought** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>2100 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Tetranadon (Target)**<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tetranadon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The original toadversary starts on all fours with the mobility to leap into the sky to Sumo Slam faraway threats and to stomp both sides if you approach. These are just two examples of how it can make an impact with the aftermath's tremors immobilising prey even if it doesn't hit the mark. The palm strikes and charging bite attack are bog water standard but one move that sets it apart is a Clap (which follows raising forelegs to both sides) since it can stun you on close impact, A second unique attack is a lunging munch attack where it attempts to grab a hunter and swallow them whole along with the dirt they stood on. The end of this move can be escaped quickly with Wirefall, so make sure you keep a Wirebug spare if possible.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/50/)

Whether it decides to eat the earth alone or does so with you included, it will eventually bloat up to the point that it can no longer do some actions but it does raise its head in the new bipedal stance. This may make maximising damage or stun more difficult even if it is a bigger target. In this state, it has a Sumo Stomp which shatters the stones to whichever side it raised and lowered its hind leg onto (huge opening as it recovers) and can dig into the ground again to hurl a boulder to throw at you. While other attacks are safe enough to block, you are likely to be waterlogged by the boulder (Use Nulberries from the supply box or Wiredash to shake off the Waterblight). There is also a water vapour tossing attack after reeling back an arm which can force a stumble even from afar from it is off-target.

It may seem like a good call to lead it away from water though you'll still need to consider a water beam attack (telegraphed by stepping back and gargling) that goes forward, sweeps across then finally vertically that can be blocked. It is also able to fire a powerful water bullet regardless of the form, so you can't fight from a distance without being on the move. Stay agile and you'll be able to make this frog croak and bite the dust for good.

* * *

| **Walking on Eggshells** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1500 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Kulu-Ya-Ku (Target)**<br>Barroth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Kulu-Ya-Ku**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

It is easy to pigeonhole the target monster as meek at a glance, but Kulu-Ya-Ku can turn the tables with its ability to dig and scavenge for a weapon with its claws. While it may leave a brief opening, the resulting small boulder comes in handy for the bird wyvern whether it is to deflect poorly aimed strikes, to smash threats or for tossing as a projectile. Mind that there is a chance that it may dig up a fragile pot that doesn't deflect regular strikes, so you don't always need to be careful and that's before considering attacks that ignore sharpness completely.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/49/)

It loses access to a few attacks when its hands are full like the claw swipes (can be done just once), but generally benefits a lot if you allow it to get a hold of anything though it will not fight with an egg in tow. The most notable moves include a leaping attack which usually follows hopping in place, a pivoting dodge and a charging smash which actually has a fairly quick recovery (can be repeated).

To get an edge against the bird that moves more like man, you should take advantage of blinding options such as the Flashfly or Flash Bombs as it will drop its precious precious and its literal guard if done successfully. You can even find a Flashfly to help out in Area 11 not far from the eggs that it likes so much.

* * *

| **Fightin' Dirty** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>2400 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Barroth (Target)**<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Barroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Normally, a monster's head is one of the most desirable points to strike if not outright even if getting in the dirt and fighting face to face exclusively is poor form. This bullish beast muddies the waters as the brute's head happens to be its most armoured spot of all, both taking less damage and bouncing back many a melee attack unless you aim to stun it. If you would like the batter the Barroth, you need to consider its tail (which can be severed) and torso, though its forelegs and hind legs are also potential targets.

The monster isn't just headstrong literally, but can also increase the range of many of its head, tail and body attacks after it stores mud by rolling around in wet patches in the Sandy Plains. While most moves fling mud bullets (which either cause Waterblight on impact with a hunter - take the Supply Nulberries - or leaves muck on the ground), two notable attacks don't get affected by this at all. One is a bull-like charge that stops short when it collides with a wall and another is when it shakes mud off to its sides which can make it difficult to fight from mid-range. This second case is an opening if you are adept from afar, happen to be close or have a gap-closing method to get some damage.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/44/)

Some other ways to break down Barroth include using Fire or Ice as well as their associated ailments (Fireblight, Blastblight and even Poison are strong), be sure to prepare an opportunity if you catch it sleeping under the mud and you may even be able to scrape it off to have a good clean hunt with the warrior of the wasteland.

* * *

| **Obnoxious Lord, Noxious Monster** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1500 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Great Wroggi (Target)**<br>Arzuros | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Wroggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The Great Wroggi is similar to the Baggi you've seen in colder climates as also fires a projectile to weaken prey and threats alike. In this case, it is poison whether through a cloudy mist or spit that travels in an arc. In both cases, it tends to back away which can be considered an additional tell of its intent but two poison attacks are quite distinct as the spit goes in an arc which can easily miss nearby hunters (not a problem for the smaller Wroggi) and the cloud is unblockable while covering a lot of ground in front of it.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/47/)

A common habit is for it to strike with its charging tackle right almost immediately after the poison cloud after aligning itself horizontally, which can chip away at your recoverable health. It is best to avoid being in the wrong place and the wrong time by circling the monster so you don't have to use too many Antidotes from the Supply Box or alternatively you can use the endemic life like the Antidobra on higher ground near Area 8. When tired, it won't be able to hurl any poison but it can still throw its weight around and you'll be able to find it sleeping in Area 11. If you manage to lead it or find it near the water of the Flooded Forest, drop some EZ Meat Bait from the Supply Box to let the Giganha take a bite out of it.

* * *

| **Spongy Oasis** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>2400 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Royal Ludroth (Target)**<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Royal Ludroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/46/)

Get as many Nulberries as you need from the Supply Box to counteract being thrashed and splashed by the Royal Ludroth and even the common Droths. While the target monster's greatest strengths lie in its mass and mastery of water, you can work to reduce both by cutting its tail and by leading it away from water areas though it will likely retreat to restore itself in Area 6 near a Waterfall. Fortunately, you can take advantage of this by using EZ Meat Bait to lure Giganhas into the fray if you are forced to fight it where its projectiles travel further.

It puts both water and weight to good use when enraged as it can charge across the area multiple times while firing water blasts, however, it also boasts water shots that get extended range if you allow it to fight in its element. Its size can prove as a weakness for it as it has difficulty with pivoting to attack threats to its side or behind it besides rolling to its side or a tail whip. It is also notable how its mane is actually more vulnerable to sharp and electric damage than its head since it is a spongy hide for retaining water. Regardless, you'll have to be aware of how it does have the ability to pivot with specific attacks with one last highlight being a spinning tail strike that follows it raising its head while shooting water to turn around.

* * *

| **Supply Run** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1200 z |
| NPC | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Felyne, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 6 Classic Dango Ingredients**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The **Fabled Spice** is in small packages all over the place, but fortunately marked on the map and automatically delivered as Account Items. There aren't any large wild monsters to worry about so it is a good time to collect an Egg for Kamura points or to focus on slaying small monsters for a Subquest.

If you are successful, the reward will include **Leg Day Dango** on the menu - perfect for a parkour pro looking to run up walls for longer.

* * *

| **Faceless Foe** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1200 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Khezu (Target)**<br>Aknosom | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Khezu**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

A battle with Khezu may be a shock as it doesn't have any music playing, but this is by nature since only get a chorus of roars till a monster sees you. On one hand, its lack of sight may give an opening to set up traps, but on the other, the monster has a habit of using massive roars to disrupt threats which gives it a chance to orient and sense you. There are means to get around the roar with well-timed defensive actions or even interrupt the monster as it lowers its head for the screech, but you will most likely be interrupted if you don't pace your approach.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/48/)

The monster's physiology is fairly standard with the head and neck taking the most damage, but the tail is especially tough as it needs to be strong to help the monster root itself to the ground, ceilings and walls. On that note, you can tell apart its electrical attacks from the behaviour of its tail as it is rooted to the ground before shooting blasts forward which are conducted along and up surfaces much like Pikachu's Thunder Jolt in Smash Bros. When the tail isn't rooted while standing on the ground, Khezu shoots a projectile into the air that arcs before landing with an explosion. This move is also done from the ceiling if the monster gets the chance to fight while in a cave, but you can use Wiredash or long-ranged attacks to knock it down when it is hanging down.

It is best to be careful as it can switch to leaping from the ceiling to attack with an electric tackle (also done on the ground) with all of its electric moves including Thunderblight (cure with Supply Box's Nulberry). In essence, defeating the Khezu is about managing your distance whether that is done through standard-issue Throwing Kunai, Wiredash actions or using slopes to allow melee attacks and resheathing to become safer. Khezu can seem impossible to reach when it gets in the habit of docking its tail below it to zap anything close like Street Fighter's Blanka but also overextends with its neck at other times which can be a big opening.

* * *

| **The Cactus Diet** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1500 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Barroth<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 10 Bauble Cactus**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

You only need to collect ten of the specific cacti which won't take too long if you follow your map while keeping in mind that they tend to be on higher ground.

* * *

| **A Sandy Cabal** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1500 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Barroth<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, **Jaggi (Target)**, **Jaggia (Target)**, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 14 Jaggi or Jaggia**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Riddle me this, what barks up and down the desert but seeks direction in life? A pack of Jaggi and Jaggia without their **Great** leader! You may cross paths with two wild monsters on your hunt, but neither will shed a wyvern tear for those who aren't their own kin.

* * *

| **Breath of the Past** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1500 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Great Wroggi<br>Royal Ludroth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 10 Simple Prisms**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

You can find them when you gather from Unique Item: Clear Crystal spots which are dotted all over your map. The wild monsters shouldn't be a problem as the Simple Prisms are in remote places including near the Pyramid of Area 3, but you can use EZ Meat Bait from the supply box if you find yourself in a pinch near the waters.

* * *

| **Ladies of the Lake** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1500 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Arzuros<br>Royal Ludroth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, **Ludroth (Target)**, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay 10 Ludroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While hunting for the Ludroths, you should take Nulberries and EZ Meat Bait from the Supply Box and mind that the king may be in the area to disrupt your plans.

* * *

| **Monkey Wrench in Your Plans** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3000 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Bishaten (Target)**<br>Arzuros<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Bishaten**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/45/)

When you find the Bishaten, you will likely find it on all fours but it won't be long before it pivots on its tail, hind legs and forelegs with each stance having its own benefits and risks which even play into how it throws projectiles. As it prefers to leap around its prey as if it were a Capoeira dancer, so it only has a few claw swipes where it attacks from in place with other attacks on all-fours including a flip to strike with its tail which leaves it stuck for a while and a wild attack that follows doing a flip backwards.

Generally, it will try to play at mid-range starting with leaping away while throwing Jumbofruit (yellow, no ailment) and Poisonfruit (purple, inflicts poison on hit - use antidote from Supply Box) from its pouch. Thanks to its prehensile tail, it can also perform additional three fruit attacks with the Jumbo one having greater range, the Poison leaving toxic mist and a Flashfruit (flashing yellow) attack blinding anyone else in the area. Mind that all three have clear tells as the fruit unique appearances and the monster treats them differently, even covering its mouth after the Poisonfruit tail attack and its eyes before and during the Flashfruit attack.

Once on its forelegs, the head is easier to reach but this stance allows it to perform the flipping tail attack multiple times with very little recovery but no ability to correct direction till it stops. From standing on its tail, there are actually two subtle stances with one allowing it to move around by spinning which can lead into a spinning top charge after it coils up and to perform a flipping tail strike rapidly but with a bit of a pause to recover which allows it to turn to chase a target. There is also a stationary tail stand which can be told apart by how its tail coils away from its body to hold the ground with the end rather than coiling inward. This stationary tail stance allows it to throw fruit with both hands-free which it may do rapidly if it isn't swaying to strike nearby threats with its wings.

If you knock the monster down, it may drop a handful of fruit ( **Poisonfruit** \- thrown poison projective, **Jumbofruit** \- heals you on use, **Flashfruit** \- thrown blinding projectile) which you can continue to use on the hunt. Mind that these items are too dangerous to keep after a hunt, so it is important to use them while you can.

* * *

| **Infernal Lacrimosa** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3600 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Somnacanth (Target)**<br>Aknosom<br>Great Baggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Somnacanth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/69/)

On your hunt for the shell-obsessed Somnacanth, the Supply Box has two items that may come in handy with one being EZ Screamer Pod which can help prevent the target from doing its sleep powder ring attack (works for some other attacks). The second item, Energy Drink, helps with the sleep ailment from its mist attacks so it shouldn't be wasted on the common or Great Baggi. While both of the other large monsters tend to fight on drier land, which makes it dangerous, it may be a good idea may be best to lure into these snowy zones (Stinkmink can help) since Somnacanth will become less powerful and even lose attacks but even rockier parts of Area 12 can work.

More specifically on how to take the Mermaid Dragon down, the weakest points to strike are its Neck, Head (not the barbed Head Fin) and Tail and you have to acknowledge how it exposes these points as it moves into different stances and attacks. Firstly, the front stroke stance allows it to cut through the water for a quick tackle, short-range talon swipes and a sleep-inducing mist beam that goes forward. Its backstroke stance allows it to pivot around the area with its forelegs free to use shell-based attacks to inflict blind and Blastblight (evade or use Deodorant to clear) but also to spurt out sleep-inducing mist on the go which can make it easier to strike with other attacks.

The last stance is "tailstand" which still allows it to pivot to get around while extending the reach of strikes with its talons greatly. Your biggest opening to counterattack is after its head fin sweep (follows it raising its head) or during its mist attacks though both of them cover a lot more surface area in this stance. One mist attack sweeps the area in front of it of the leviathan but another starts with its neck emitting mist as it raises its head before mist sweeps the area in all directions. While the monster is more skilled in inflicting ailments than brutal force, you shouldn't overlook how it can leap from the tailstand into the air to tackle a foe while also landing Waterblight.

* * *

| **Raging White-Out** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3600 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Barioth (Target)**<br>Great Izuchi<br>Somnacanth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/53/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Barioth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The amber fanged Barioth may seem bulky and ill-suited to the climate at a glance, but actually has an easy time clinging onto and reorienting on the ice in the area thanks to its winged forelegs. Be sure to break them to slow down the monster for an easier fight though the head is a weaker weak point for sharp, blunt and gun damage. On a related note, its tail can be severed to take the icy wind out of Barioth's sails while being a weak point as well.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/63/)

As far as its attacks, it can spit out an ice projectile that spawns an ice cyclone (use supply Nulberries for Iceblight) even if it is blocked (launches on hit) but is otherwise fairly linear with a short-range Hip Check and standard leaps. Like mentioned before, it is quite mobile due to its ice movement but it can also flap its wings to reposition or to escape backwards after being thrashed (try cornering it). You should expect to have to work for openings against this one which can be helped by waiting for it to eat Anteka in Area 6 for an ambush or by making an unlikely alliance with Somnacanth or the Izuchi.

When you eventually enrage the monster, you'll be able to tell from its quicker movements and red glowing eyes rather than blue as well as a powerful leap attack where it rams into the earth after stepping back and raising its head. When this attack misses, you get one of the bigger openings you may get from the monster as it can flap into the air for distance, though a well-placed Flash Bomb (not included as Supply) can change that.

* * *

| **The Queen's Procession** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3000 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Rathian (Target)**<br>Basarios<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rathian**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While your target may be known as the "Queen of the Land", Her Highness can also roam through the skies for a moment as the royal wyvern either attacks or switches to a flying stance, both allow the Rathian to inflict poison in a flash with a tail flip with it being the primary attack from the air with the second attack being it flying into a faraway threat. While this provides an opening as it has to land slowly to recover, the monster is able to flip multiple times without landing when enraged (see the flames from its mouth). In the case, you are hit **you can count on an antidote from the Supply Box**.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/68/)

On that note, **the box also has EZ Flash Bombs** which can be used to down it while it is in flight. While the flashes become less effective if it is downed multiple times, it is still a good opportunity to go for the head, wings and tail (which can be severed to shorten the Tail Flip's range). Other attacks of interest include its fire breath which is fairly standard between a single fireball, triple fireballs and a high impact fireball and its running tackle that can be repeated many times when enraged.

* * *

| **Streaking Shadow** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3600 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Tobi-Kadachi (Target)**<br>Bishaten<br>Great Wroggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tobi-Kadachi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While no part of the jumpy Tobi-Kadachi is clearly clad in scales, the weakest point to strike is the large tail though its head, neck and torso and back are fine alternatives. It is highly mobile but very vulnerable to ailments with one example being poison which can be inflicted by Poison Kunai from the Supply Box, endemic life like Poisonfrogs, the Great Wroggi but also the Poisonfruit that Bishaten can drop. It is also worth finding a way to inflict Waterblight as this prevents your target from charging up into a second form, though simply using EZ Meat Bait (or less easy substitutes) in the Giganha-infested waters can slow it down.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/70/)

After Tobi-Kadachi jumpstarts itself into its charged form (it happens after raising its tail), it can even inflict Thunderblight from a bite (use the supply Nulberries) besides gaining the ability to glide in the air to do two attacks in the air after going forward. One attack is linear; a tail flip attack that only strikes ahead of it but it can also perform a spinning whip to both sides which can be told apart by how its tail bends to the side or alternatively how the head reaches the ground first before the whip happens. You have more of an opening for after the spin, but it has a similar but grounded flip which gives a decent opening.

When Tobi-Kadachi decides to hang on a wall to catch a breather, it doesn't have a lot of options from there if it hasn't charged up already and you should find a way to set a trap, strike it in the air or use long-range if you don't have a Flash Bomb.

* * *

| **A Song of Red and Fire** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>2400 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Volvidon (Target)**<br>Great Wroggi<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Volvidon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This hunt revolves around following Volvidon's habits as it effectively doesn't have a "weak side" that it can't reach on short notice, unlike many other monsters. It can also cause **two nasty ailments** whether you are in front of it or around it. The first ailment comes from the Stench that it scatters around it while on all-fours with a brown gas in all directions but towards its face, it is so rancid that any afflicted hunter needs to use **Deodorant** (supplied by the guild in the box for this quest) before they can stomach any consumable items - mind that it is blockable if need be. The second ailment is **paralysis from the toxin that spits out while bipedal, there is no cure for this** if it connects properly but an ally can launch you which can get you out of a pitch and shake it off.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/71/)

The monster also has range thanks to its long tongue to snapping at threats in front but also has burst options as it can often coiling into a sphere to roll backwards, forward, sideways and even drift at times. Generally, it is difficult to punish the ball beast with melee while it is using the rolling attacks, but one of them has it leap into the sky before landing on its back. This is a rare case where you can move in as it unfurls forward to all-fours for the head, the weakest part of the monster though it is worth noting that the back (the zigzagging part) and front upper half of the monster are also good targets to maximise impact. In a pitch, you can tack on damage with standard-issue kunai if it is bouncing around and other tactics can't be interrupted by a roar, at least not by this monster.

* * *

| **Off Your Rocker** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3000 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Basarios (Target)**<br>Khezu<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Basarios**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The gas-blasting Basarios can be tough to tackle both due to its heavy plating and due to its ability to spew both poisonous gas and flames from its body. Fortunately, you can have some Supply Box items to address this with the explosive **EZ Large Barrel Bombs** to break up the shell on its abdomen and Antidotes for poison, but the Fireblight may have to be addressed by running, dropping and rolling. That being said, most of the monster is clad in stone which leaves the tail tip, head, legs and abdomen as the few safe spots to strike with regular melee attacks - also giving more damage.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/64/)

Generally, the monster has linear and obvious attacks but ones that can cover a lot of distance so be sure to read when it will roll out of a run which leaves its head exposed low to the ground, it also does the roll from neutral following raising its head. It may also skid out of a run, but you usually have enough time to recognise its chosen attack option including its gas-based attacks with one interesting one being when it steps forward with flaming gas. In the case that you can't get a good angle to strike, it may be better to focus on long-range to tack on damage, though you have to be aware that while it isn't the most mobile monster that it can actually pivot when it digs into the ground and can strike with its tail (the tip can be severed - mostly for the carve) while walking backwards.

If you want a better chance to break down the bellowing boulder beast, you may want to take control of Khezu for Thunderblight since your target has poor resistance to all elements and many ailments (sleep, water and blast especially) despite the tough shell.

* * *

| **Getting Back the Groceries** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>2100 z |
| NPC | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Basarios<br>Volvidon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 6 Secret Dango Ingredients**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This is a standard gathering quest with the target being six **Legendary Herbs** that can be found in **Small Packages** dotted high and low around the Lava Caverns. If you collect the account items by following your map, you'll end up with a **Dango Ticket** in your Bonus Rewards for the good work.

* * *

| **A Poisonous Project** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3000 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Pukei-Pukei (Target)**<br>Bishaten | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Pukei-Pukei**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

As you pursue the poison-spewing Pukei-Pukei, it is best to take the Antidote supplies from the box as your target is toxic from back to front and to consider using the EZ Meat Bait in waters with Giganha. This winged wyvern does have telltale signs of when it will use poison attacks which you can even see from how its mouth and tail may be swollen after a meal, this is a sign that poisonous spit may spread, linger or rise up as a persistent gas. This gives more layers to anticipate how the battle may go if you recognise where and what the herbs or mushrooms are in the area, perhaps setting a trap to call out it out after it looks around and slobbers with its great tongue.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/67/)

On that note, the tongue is one of its greatest assets for extending its range with a straight stab as well as consecutive swipes but it is especially sensitive to damage and happens to be tied to attacks with higher recovery even after retracting. Similarly, it can shoot streams of poison from its tail across and skyward at a velocity that launches hunters from the sheer force, but the end can be severed to lower its range even for physical attacks. If you want a more general plan, you can focus on using thunder and ice attacks with the main ailments being paralysis, sleep and Thunderblight to take it down.

* * *

| **Deliver the Liver** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3150 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Basarios<br>Volvidon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 5 Monster Guts**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you want to deliver the Boozy Youth's request, you'll need to head north to Areas 9 and 10 where the Delex swim in the sand. After you splatter their guts and collect five bunches of em, they will be delivered as account items so you won't have to return to camp. That being said, the smaller monsters are pretty evasive which can make it difficult to avoid being hit during a carve let alone catching them. Be sure to use the EZ Screamer Pod or any spare Sonic Bombs to get them out of the sand, though a Flash alternative ( **Flashflies are in both Areas**) can work if they are looking the right way.

Don't spare too many resources as they will often come to you and give you a chance to stun them or even cluster together making a Sonic Bomb super effective.

* * *

| **So Hot, It Melts Iron** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3150 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Great Wroggi<br>Khezu | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this GATHERING quest is to **deliver 12 pieces of Coal**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The coal can be mined from within and outside of the Lava Caverns at **Scorched Ore Unique Items** rather than your usual mining outcrop. You'll normally find it near to some magma from within the burning mountain or lava that leaks outside of it, so watch your step on the way there.

* * *

| **THE BEST Quest** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>2100 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Somnacanth<br>Volvidon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Remobra, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, **Rachnoid (Target)** |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay 10 Rachnoid**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

On your quest for THE BEST, you may want to follow the summary by collecting thread for the client but the objective is just to slay 10 Rachnoid with the carving part not even being needed for an account item. This means their carves are all yours whether it is Rachnoid Silk, Monster Fluid or their Sharp Claws, but you should mind the Rachnoid's flames since they will flare up regardless of whether it is a hunter or large monster that poses a threat.

* * *

| **Third Wheel** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Arena | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3000 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Arzuros (Target)**<br>**Tetranadon (Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Your goal in this ENDURANCE quest is to **hunt all target monsters**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

During endurance quests, you have to take on multiple monsters but they either arrive separately in the assigned locale after some time has passed. This means that you'll be able to tackle Arzuros alone but also can't force it or Tetranadon to hit each other till later on so time and resource management can be a problem. For that reason, you should collect the Rainbow Spiribird and also the Supply Box (EZ Flash Bombs and Nulberries) before leaping into the Arena but this Locale actually has some bonuses if you need some room to guarantee a capture (takes less time than slaying) without the wrong monster stumbling in a trap.

The Arena will allow you to **raise the Fence** moments after Tetranadon arrives which can be helpful to get away some room from both monsters (at the expense of time - it gets lowered on a timer) or to force specific matchups, but you should be familiar enough that it isn't needed too much. Neither target is in their element since Arzuros is far from honey and Tetranadon can't make use of its water palm strike.

* * *

| **Comeuppance** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4800 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Magnamalo (Target)**<br>Great Izuchi<br>Rathian | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt Magnamalo**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

After being isolated after its rampaging feast, here you face the barbarous beast. Back again in the familiar Shrine Ruins, you'll find Deodorant in the Supply Box which can help with Hellfireblight which cause your hunter or buddies to explode after being hit again or after a certain time. It is key to pace your use of this as you can **Wiredash (not Wirefall)** to leave the hellfire bomb behind you which can actually catch Magnamalo off-guard though should not take this blight lightly as it makes Palamutes unable to give you a ride till they recover.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/66/)

To have a good fighting chance against the flagship, you should recognise that Magnamalo can roar before doing a second one with explosive force and focus on its weak points. This mainly means the head for sharp/blunt attacks, while the Tailblade (tail tip) and Armblades (golden parts on forelegs) but you may notice them being enveloped in flame at times which is a sign that they are sensitive but have access to extended attacks with Hellfire. Still, on weaknesses, using water and thunder is your best shot but if you don't have access to that you can work with poison that Rathian can inflict with its tail flip.

The Magnamalo does have a second form with brighter magenta flames (Magentamalo? Whatever we call it there is yet another form with its spikes raised) though it is important to focus on how it distributes its flames first and foremost. When you see the flames on its Tailblade means that its stinger attack will leave hellfire vapour which will explode after a while and them being on its arms mean that it striking the earth in front of it will also make it explode. This makes it a better idea to retreat directly away after it lines its head and tail together for the Stinger if it hasn't gotten the hellfire charge on the tail, but it is still generally better to circle around the beast so you can get better chances to strike weak points. On the note of the tail, your target's intent can be told from how it is positioned since it whirls its tail before the Hellfire Cannon. Other examples include how the monster will usually wait till it has flicked its tail following any moves which leave vapour (note the exceptions like the next paragraph) and how the arcs for hellfire balls can't cover near opponents if you can slip by while it is walking or standing.

While magenta, Magnamalo's magnum opus has it flip backwards, roar with hellfire vapour seeping around it before tackling into the player to launch them before divebombing with all of its might, though it can also perform the divebomb alone after a smaller roar and step back. It can be difficult to time a dive-evade (commonly called the "Superman Dive", a weaponless running evade away from a large monster - it has full invincibility) for the full attack, but there is also the option to block as long as you have the stamina and health to take it. In the worst-case scenario, you can use a Wirefall before the divebomb part when launched though you have to time it properly with the correct direction. A more limited alternative is you can use Flash Bombs to disrupt the monster which can create a big opening depending on when it catches the Wynern of Malice.

If you keep the Spirit of Kamura aflame, you'll receive the **Ancestral Blade award** and the **Hand-Me-Down Sword I** **(Long Sword)** after the credits.

* * *

| **Wind Speed Wyvern** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4200 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Nargacuga (Target)**<br>Arzuros<br>Rathalos | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/57/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/58/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Nargacuga**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The lean, mean Nargacuga doesn't specialise in inflicting ailments, however, it is pretty tricky to down due to its speed and excellent reach from all sides. The weak point is its head (sharp and blunt), but you'll have to keep an eye on its tail (the tip can be severed - to shorten range) as well as its "Cutwings" that are tough, deflective parts connected to the forearms though they are especially frail against fire and electric attacks. For that reason, you may want to bring explosives or draw Rathalos towards the pouncing shadow for a Turf War.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/132/)

Otherwise, the jet-black beast is renowned for its tail strikes as it can crouch down to ready a tail whip that can cross a surprising range but it also has a spinning tail attack which usually follows it raising its head with its mouth open. The Cutwing attacks are also key to recognise as the monster recovers from them quicker in addition to them being tough to break, but you can throw off Narga's balance if you follow through. When Nargacuga is enraged with red glowing eyes, you can expect some new attacks like it rattling its tail before sending tailspikes your way and a sneaky tail slam that follows it walking slowly with its body to the ground.

* * *

| **Electrifying Ephiphany** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4500 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Zinogre (Target)**<br>Great Wroggi<br>Pukei-Pukei | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/62/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Zinogre**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The proud Zinogre can be hard to pin down as it dances during battles between firing electric projectiles and faking out before charging up to reach another form which is mixed faster and slower attacks. This is all the more reason to be a good dance partner by following the tells for when you can attack safely. Some key examples in its normal state have the Lord of Lightning charge electricity from a step (recognise the sparks before the foreleg lands) or backstep spin for a small opening, though you can get more if you catch the monster after a spinning tail strike (note the tail over its head and backstep) or tail flip.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/134/)

As far as weak points, you should mainly be focusing on the head, back, hind leg and hip with ice, water and fire elements (poison can also be effective - consider using the other wild monsters) if possible. While Zinogre's tough tail tip can be a problem, it shouldn't be an epiphany to find that you can sever it off if you cut close to the base. If you do this, you may have less to worry about when it goes into its **Supercharged State (directly out of a charge with a hitbox - stay back)** though it will still be able to inflict Thunderblight with the step attacks from before to inflict stun quickly (use Nulberries from the supply box to recover).

While Zinogre is more fierce in its final form with a new attack pattern, it is key to note that prior attacks are still present with similar punishes being relevant though one attack stands out when it lands on its back since there is a delayed lightning strike which makes it harder to punish but offers counter opportunities. Additionally, you can knock it back to normal with enough pressure. From there, it may be desperate to recharge and backspin to get an opening to do so towards the end of a fight.

* * *

| **Nocturnal Tracker** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4200 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Anjanath (Target)**<br>Basarios<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Anjanath**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

To bully the brute wyvern, you may want to force them to fight someone their own size like Basarios but ultimately, you'll have the size up with the Anjanath directly. This will involve finding a way to reach its weak points (head, snout, wings and tail) since they only end up being close to the ground for brief windows. You'll want to recognise its habits by watching its head since it usually bites once after turning but can bite three times while gaining ground (five when enraged) which makes it predictable. When Anjanath raises its head while running forward, it is trying to increase torque as it swings its head and often oversteps the target with the wide attack, though a lowered stance with the top of its skull facing you means it will charge with all its might.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/130/)

Generally, your target has a bit of a naff time with turning but it will be resourceful enough to try to flick at foes at its heels and to whip any foes directly behind it and far away though it can leap a great distance while causing tremors and toss a rock with its jaws. If you roil up and enrage the monster, you'll see its wings spread with its snout becomes enflamed before it can do a series of more attacks. Examples include breathing a beam of fire, a 180-degree tail whip while causing a gust that can trip hunters (unless it is tired) and a wild double bite charge attack.
