This last attack can have the monster turn slightly between the bites and juggle its prey in such a way that it can catch bad Wirefalls, so be sure to circle around the monster if you see it lower its head with the jaws open.

* * *

| **Waltzing by Moonlight** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4500 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Mizutsune (Target)**<br>Arzuros<br>Zinogre | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/62/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Mizutsune**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The fox wyvern regularly sends a wave of bubbles which makes it harder to read where you position yourself, especially as the white bubbles cause Bubbleblight that cause the afflicted party to slip and slide (use a Cleanser from the supply box or roll around to recover). While there are also positive bubbles to touch directly (attack-boosting red & healing green) and you can even burst bubbles in flight with kunai, you shouldn't lose sight of the main threat.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/131/)

As for catching the bubble beast, Mizutsune can be a slippery one though you can take note of its tells before major attacks like how it raises its claw while on its hind legs before a claw strike with great reach, but notable recovery. Things do change if you let the fated fox get angry and spin in a circle to lather up while releasing bubbles that orbit around it. Following this, the monster will be able to glide across the area with ease even while spinning with a pressured water beam (escape to higher ground or Wiredash above) but also be able to skate around to claw at you as you are avoiding or pressuring bubbles from even more angles than before.

You can take advantage of its habit of always being around water by using EZ Meat Bait to interrupt its flow, but may want to consider getting Zinogre to clash with your target since its Mounted Punisher **inflicts Thunderblight** which can make it easier to stun and stop the bubble hell. On that note, the weak points are the head, dorsal fin, neck, tail and hind leg in that order with the forelegs and claws being tougher but especially sensitive to electric or ice damage.

* * *

| **Rathalos Alert!** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4500 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Rathalos (Target)**<br>Great Wroggi<br>Magnamalo | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/58/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Remobra, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rathalos**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Much like Rathian, your target's emerald counterpart of the land, Rathalos has two stances with one being on the ground and the other soaring the skies. While His Highness prefers to the air, it is worth noting its key attacks from the ground as it has some unique habits like a nasty double bite from standing which can easily catch bad Wirefalls (also inflicts Fireblight when enraged) but it can also flap its wings to hop in place for a small stomp or flap away a bit to shoot a fireball. Generally, it is best to keep the monster on the ground since the double tail whip and most fire attacks are predictable, but when it flaps into the air especially when enraged with flames seeping from its mouth things change.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/133/)

From the air, Rathalos can fly by and rain down frying fireballs before gliding to attack and land, strike with its tail to whip to the side before landing, shoot three fireballs before landing, dive kick from across the area **(inflicts poison - use the supply Antidote)**, glide kick before doing other attacks and more. One key thing to consider most attacks from the air and the ground have the monster facing towards its prey, this makes the supply box's EZ Flash Bomb effective at grounding it immediately for a large opening.

As far as the continued effectiveness of the Flash Bomb, when you come at the King of the Skies, you best not miss since Rathalos eventually builds up resistance to the point it only makes him pause and need to land down slowly which can be an opportunity in itself if you have traps, Flashbugs or Flash Bombs. This is a champion time to strike weak points like the head, tail (can be severed) and tail especially with electric or water attacks. Do mind that while turf wars and clashes between monsters are usually also an opening, but that Magnamalo will make sure that you feel its divebomb as well if you get too close.

* * *

| **The Secret Ingredient** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4680 z |
| NPC | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Somnacanth (Target)**<br>Barioth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/53/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this CAPTURE quest is to **capture a Somnacanth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

In the Frost Islands, there are massive spans of water that help the Somnacanth swim quickly and even use shocking shell attacks with burning and blinding properties. For that reason, you may want to draw the target to drier parts of those areas (if not other areas entirely with Stinkmink - can be found at eastern Area 6 entrance). Don't sleep on guaranteeing that you have the space to dodge Somnacanth's strikes and sleep powder for a more straightforward hunt.

While the supply box has some essential trap and tranq items besides an Energy Drink, it is best to bring some of your own supplies as well if you want to taste sweet victory. On which note, your bonus rewards will include a **Dango Ticket** which sweetens the deal with **Invigorating Dango** being made available to order, thanks to Somnacanth's song as inspiration.

* * *

| **BZZZZZ or ZZZZzzzz** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4560 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Khezu (Target)**<br>**Somnacanth (Target)**<br>Magnamalo | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Khezu and a Somnacanth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you don't want to be zapped or be forced to take a nap, you can take some precautions in addition to the Supply Box (Energy Drink, Nulberries and EZ Screamer Pod) and mind the Magnamalo. Khezu usually bends or sparks with electricity even before it can attack while Somnacanth has distinct clicks and cries for its shell and sleep dust attacks, so it is fairly easy to read their intent but both can stop in you tracks with ailments.

* * *

| **Nosey Nuisances** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5220 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Kulu-Ya-Ku (Target)**<br>**Anjanath (Target)**<br>Pukei-Pukei | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Kulu-Ya-Ku and an Anjanath**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

As a hunter of Kamura that knows no bounds, you should already be targetting the head of both targets by instinct. Mind that they have different ways of defending their weak point with Anjanath's towering frame or whatever Kulu's lucky rock.

* * *

| **The Hottest Around** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4200 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Tetranadon (Target)**<br>**Volvidon (Target)**<br>Rathalos | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/58/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tetranadon and a Volvidon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

With the theme of this quest is Speed and Weight, you'll have to stay sharp to avoid being caught off guard by their more tricky actions (Tetranadon's stunning clap and Volvidon's paralysing spit - its stench attack has Deodorant in the Supply Box). They may feel more comfortable when bloated and rolling respectively, but a big impact will bring them around. While it may be inefficient without a Puppet Spider or Stinkmink, you can try counting on Rathalos if you want to bring some heat.

* * *

| **Hermit of the Swamp** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5100 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Almudron (Target)**<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku<br>Nargacuga | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/57/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Almudron**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

As you get down in the dirt, you should take note that the monster's weak points are its **severable** tail tip (to sharp damage) and its head (to blunt and gun attacks) though the whole tail is a great target too. Almudron has two general stances to consider, one standing on all fours with its tail behind and another where it is swimming with its abdomen submerged in the earth and tail in front. The monster is able to move with relative ease, but mainly when it dives out of sight which gives you a bit of time to retreat if you anticipate something big.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/129/)

Since "mud" is in the middle of its name, it is no surprise that it even creates mud platforms passively which can both hinder a hunter by launching them or blocking paths but also give new aerial approaches. While on all fours, the monster can flick mud in aggressive formations including muck bullets forward but also sweeping around it to make a wave that causes Waterblight ( **Wiredash to shake it off or bring Nulberries**) but also leaves muck as it settles. It can also strike with great force with its tail with it is with a stronger range punch after coiling its body or after turning and raising its hind legs to slam on anything in the way. Almudron's tail also gives it the ability to pivot and reach far when striking with its forelegs, though it is pretty telegraphed as it is the only case that has the monster raises the front half of its body.

The swimming stance is another story that starts with it dipping into the mud but always slamming its tail down. Mind that **the monster only slams its tail down from the initial dive**, not following it leaping out of the mud into another part of the area and that both swimming and standing stances leave the weakest points exposed. If you manage to enrage the monster, its body will change colour, attack faster and the mud in many attacks will turn gold. Not all that glitters is golden as the muck will burn any prey that touches it, so it becomes especially important to have options to escape upwards and away. The **angry acidic Almudron** will also change from doing the double foreleg strike to doing a foreleg strike followed by a tail whip that sends muck.

During the battle, you may see it spinning around before diving into the mud to form a boulder that attaches to its tail with mucus; it does stop the monster from being able to move as freely and removes the mud flicking but also makes an impact by making the standing tail punch more of a wrecking boulder. Mind that the monster can and will leap into the air to slam a threat with Waterblight, even though its backside becomes an even larger weak point from a distance. If you stay on its tail to the end, it may make its **own domain of muck in Area 10** with massive columns of mud to blocking easy approaches with a habit of slapping away threats while submerged. It is key to recognise that the monster is usually in danger as it recovers out of the mud, but it can also flip out of it while enraged.

* * *

| **Abominable Snow-beast** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5100 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Goss Harag (Target)**<br>Lagombi<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/55/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Goss Harag**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Goss Harag is a cold-blooded beast that can equally throw its mass into any prey, chill it to the bone or do both at the same time. Fire can help with taking down the beast, but you'll need to bring your own supplies as no wild monsters will help here. On that note, the Supply Box has Nulberries that can help with the Iceblight and should focus on specific exposed weak spots (Ice Clump, Head and Forelegs) as the battle goes on. The furry monster is highly mobile, so you will have to be cautious of when it jumps into the air to slam the ground as it creates a forward column of rubble that can launch a hunter, but it is also an attack that Goss Harag recovers from quickly enough to fire an ice beam follow up whenever it is enraged.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/136/)

This ice beam will go vertically up before across before the monster switches its tactics by forming either one or two ice blades (called Ice Clumps in your Hunter's Notes). This extends the reach of its claw swipes and automatically sends projectiles that cause Iceblight, changes around a bipedal running punch for it slamming or stabbing the ground (multiple times when two ice blades are present - it recovers quickly from the stab so retreat if that's the case). Fortunately, the red flaring parts when enraged are actually weak points and the weakest of all are the ice clumps on its forelegs due to how they are fused with its body. You can break them to down the monster for a big opening but will have to slip through the frost and stone that it digs up to do so. Two attacks that give particularly big openings are when it recovers from the running punch without any ice blades (distinct as it runs on two legs with one hand behind) and when it does five swipes with its double ice blades when it returns to normal, but you may be able to take advantage of it eating Anteka and sleeping in Area 6 as well.

* * *

| **Thundering Voice** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5100 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Tigrex (Target)**<br>Barioth<br>Great Baggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/60/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/53/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tigrex**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The supply box has two items of interest that can slow down the fierce aggression of your target, **one is Tinged Meat which can paralyse a hungry Tigrex** but only once before it resists that side-effect entirely (though it eating is as an opening on face value whether in Area 5 or wherever you like). The **EZ Flash Bomb** works as you'd expect with the monster being dazed briefly before spinning in place or leaping imagined targets which makes it key to learn its tells and traits. It starts with its roar which is not only brutally loud, but powerful enough to hurt hunters that linger too close to its front. So it is important to have an exit strategy or plan of defence/counter-attack when the beast spreads its forelegs or becomes enraged (clear from red veins and red eyes).

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/139/)

The cornerstone of Tigrex's attacks is its Wild Charge where it runs forward with its mouth open but can pivot to do it again, it may seem rudimental but the monster can mix up its approach by ending the second charge by stopping then backing off or by skidding then doing its iconic spinning tail attack. You can take advantage of the wild charge by leading it into breakable rocks and structures in the area where it will get stuck or by punishing the spinning tail strike since it is one of the highest recovery attacks that the monster has. On that note, it can spin without running which is one of its best attacks for clearing threats from all sides, be sure to recognise when its foreleg wings are pointing up with the tail swaying if you want to see it coming. Be sure to focus on the head, foreleg, tail base and tail (can be severed) especially with water or electric attacks if you want an easier time.

* * *

| **Twisted Desire** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5100 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Diablos (Target)**<br>Almudron<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/54/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Diablos**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While your target is a herbivore that mainly feeds on cacti in Area 9, you are in for a diabolical time if you treat it like the common Kelbi. Diablos's speciality is its ability to dig into the sand which makes it unreachable by normal attacks till it leaps out, if you use **the Supply Box's EZ Screamer Pods** (or Sonic Bombs that Rondine may have in her rare finds to sell) against its sensitive hearing - you will be able to force it out of the ground in a trapped-like state unless it is enraged (note the black mist near its mouth). In any case, the monster leaves a big opening to be attacked even when it isn't interrupted by high-frequency sounds, but you should remember that **its dig attack while enraged has high tracking as it leaps from the sand towards a target before doing a double horn lunge**.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/135/)

Shifting to its attacks on the ground, Diablos can actually do the **double horn lunge** regardless of whether it is angry which maintains the same tracking between hits that catches poor Wirefalls that aren't high enough to escape. You can tell that the monster will do this from how it steps back and winds back its head. Another horn related duo of attacks to consider are its horn charge (ends with skidding) and horn launch (ends by raising head) where Diablos bends its wings and head down before charging forwards horns first. Both attacks are easy to avoid but it is even better if **you lead it into the ant hills** **so the hardheaded monster can be trapped into the quicksand pits**.

There are other attacks of note like the Hip Check which gets range extended by the tail, but you'll have less to worry about there if you focus on severing the tail (which affects the tail slam, tail double swing and double spin) as the club-like tailcase at the tip is massive. For specific places to hit, the abdomen, head, wing and tail are especially good particularly if you have water or ice attacks. The Mounted Punisher of Almudron can help reduce the need for sharpness through Waterblight as well since the horns and tail tip are pretty tough to crack.

* * *

| **A Test of Courage** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7400 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Magnamalo (Target)**<br>**Zinogre (Target)**<br>Mizutsune | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/62/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to hunt **Magnamalo and a Zinogre**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

In a battle between both the embodiments of Hellfireblight and Thunderblight, hunters need courage and wisdom if they want to power through the flagships. Firstly, the Supply Box contains both Deodorant and Nulberry which can keep the blighting ailments at bay, but it is preferable to Wiredash (not Wirefall) after being hit by hellfire so you can set a trap from it. Secondly, you can capture them to avoid running low on time if need be and using Flash Bombs/Flashflies can slow down all monsters on this hunt.

Other ways to stay resourceful include using clashes between the titans (they aren't allies) in additional to Trapbugs (in Area 1 near arch, between Area 7 & 8) since they reappear regularly in the same spot and cause a consistently high amount of hitstun rather than having decaying utility like most things tied to ailments. If you brave the storm, you may find a **Commendation** in your Quest Bonus Rewards.

* * *

| **Like a Flash of Lightning** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7020 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Tobi-Kadachi (Target)**<br>**Mizutsune (Target)**<br>Almudron | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tobi-Kadachi and a Mizutsune**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Stepping into the foaming feud between turbulent Tobi and maddened Mizu at a senior hunter's request can be troublesome, but you have the option to capture both, isolate the combatants and use them against each other if you perceive their tricky movements. You can take advantage of how Tobi-Kadachi is unable to charge when Waterblighted and uses some electric attacks, while Mizutsune has water attacks while hating thunder or look into the Endemic Life to complete the quest.

The supply box should not be everlooked as well, since it includes a cornucopia of items ranging from the Flooded Forest fish food to items to counteract Bubble and Thunderblights to Poison Kunai which are especially effective on Tobi-Kadachi. Avoid the floating bubbles and flying sparks and you may end up passing with flying colours with a **Commendation** in your Quest Bonus Rewards.

* * *

| **King of the Sky, Bane of the Land** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7680 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Rathalos (Target)**<br>**Tigrex (Target)**<br>Anjanath | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/58/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/60/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rathalos and a Tigrex**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

In this quest, the Supply Box has a nice range to make things EZier from the EZ Flash Bomb best used to bring the king down to earth, to tinged meat which can paralyse the monsters when they at their lowest and lastly Antidote which only has a use defensively. As far as surviving the wrath of Rathalos and the tenacity of Tigrex, your goal should be to limit their reach with tail attacks first which will increase the window to counterattack.

That being said, you still need to mind the mixups from Tigrex's angry charge on the ground and Rathalos from the sky. If you show that hunters are the bane of monsters, you may find a **Commendation in your Quest Bonus Rewards**.

* * *

| **Advanced: The Veterans' Gala** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Arena | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>17640 z |
| NPC | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Rajang (Target)**<br>**Bazelgeuse (Target)**<br>**Magnamalo (Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/145/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/143/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) |
| Your goal in this ENDURANCE quest is to **hunt all target monsters**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This challenging hunt pits you against two monsters that normally invade High Rank as well as the one and the only Magnamalo, but begins with you facing only Rajang at the start which helps with being able to slip through its legs to strike its golden tail, but that freedom won't last long. Before dropping into the arena, you'll find the safe zone does have a Rainbow Spiribird and some Supply essentials (EZ Farcaster, Nulberries and Deodorant) but that it is **worth adding to your arsenal with traps and tranqs**.

You'll find that all three monsters have great range which can end up with some friendly fire that gives hunters openings, besides the ability for the first two monsters to inflict blights on one another (Rajang being very effective on Bazel), but shouldn't always allow open chaos to continue if you either want to maximise on a knockdown, KO or stun. That's where the fence helps with your offensive.

Along with Magnamalo, all three maintain their High Rank moveset (though the Subquest ignores this) so you may want to consult this guide for tips on how to deal with them separately or recognise their habits in their specific hunts. If you prove your worth as a hero, you'll get a **High Commendation, gain the Fan of Ascendancy Award and become able to forge the Flame Seal at the Smithy**.

* * *

| **Shady Monster** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>3240 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Aknosom ( **Target**)<br>Arzuros<br>Great Wroggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Aknosom**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This bird beast has a habit of standing on a single leg which is simultaneously a show of style and strength as its stances allude to a series of attacks that are forecast further by the shape of its umbrella-shaped crest on its head. From close range, it is capable of performing two wide sweeps with its wings, a trio of pecks into the earth which can make it harder to punish due to the wind from the impact blowing you away from the crest and even a charging crest attack where it dashes wildly to strike anyone in its way but also can circle around.

This may convince you to consider fighting from a distance, but that has its own risks as it can shoot fireballs through its flammable venom which can bounce across the land and control space. There is even a fireworks-esque spectacle that it can create both from the ground and after rising slightly into the air, so it is more ideal to draw it to fight you where there is water especially as it even makes it easier to clear any Fireblight not just stopping spreading flames.

Striking the monster from behind or an angle while being on the move is a safe bet but you do have to keep some attacks in mind like a backward kick, a backstep kick that sweeps up rumble and a hop that leads to a flash kick. It is also notable that the tip of its tail is especially tough, so you may just bounce off and get punished if you are using the wrong blademaster attack in the wrong place at the wrong time.

As you progress, you may find that it often flies away after being thrashed too much but you can stop it from retreating by using blinding tactics (Flashflies for example). If you notice that it is in Area 8 after a beatdown, rush there to potentially punish it during a meal. You also have notable openings to strike following its flipping flash kick, during its triple fireball attack and after its running charge especially if it loses balance.

* * *

| **Tired and Feathered** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>2880 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku ( **Target**)<br>Aknosom<br>Arzuros | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Kulu-Ya-Ku**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

You might pigeonhole the target monster as meek at a glance, but the Kulu-Ya-Ku is capable of turning the tables with its ability to dig and scavenge with its claws. While it may leave a brief opening to get ready, a small boulder comes in handy for the bird wyvern whether it is to deflect poorly aimed strikes, to smash you or to use as a projective.

It loses access to a few attacks when its hands are full like the claw swipes (can be done just once), but generally benefits a lot if you allow it to get a hold of anything though it will not fight with an egg in tow. The most notable moves include a leaping attack which usually follows hopping in place, a pivoting dodge and a charging smash which actually has a fairly quick recovery (can be repeated).

To get an edge against the bird that moves more like man, you should take advantage of blinding options such as the Flashfly or Flash Bombs as it will drop its precious precious and its literal guard if done successfully. You can even find a Flashfly to help out near its sleeping place (Area 3) towards the tail feather end of the fight.

* * *

| **Jumping in the Frost Islands** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Frost Islands | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>1800 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Great Izuchi ( **Target**)<br>Aknosom<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Izuchi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The target monster patrols around the southern areas of the Frost Islands with two of its less great Izuchi kin joining it, they even move to other areas as a group so it is rare that you'll find the Great Izuchi alone for long. In any case, the monster only has mostly linear attacks with even its regular tail sweep having a blind spot if you are too close and low enough and other attacks often stabbing into the ground and getting stuck.

You primarily have to worry about Tail Sweep Combo (cries with its head and tail raised before acting), Flipping Stab (cries with tail raised behind) which has surprisingly great reach as it leaps forward and the risk of allowing it to do this as a pack which can control a lot of space perhaps forcing you to block for an extended time or to counter. Fortunately, being aware of the tells will help you to avoid being locked down.

* * *

| **No Cure for the Common Baggi** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Frost Islands | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>1800 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Great Baggi ( **Target**)<br>Great Izuchi<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Baggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

However, if you need a cure for the great or common tranquilising spit attack, you've got Energy Drinks from the Supply Box at the start and possibly allies who can knock you awake. The bulky Baggi differs from the lean Izuchi in how it tends to strike threats with its full weight with a charging tackle (watch for it bracing its body horizontally) and a double tail sweep which can make you stumble if it hits you.

One more distinguishing element is the habit of working more independently from its smaller kin which makes a battle with a full pack more unpredictable especially with them being able to hurl sleep-inducing spit at lower angles, unlike the adult Baggi which usually backs away to get the right arc. This makes it important to isolate it when possible so it is easy to read its intent like the powerful leap which is telegraphed by it stepping back.

* * *

| **A Snowball's Chance...** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Frost Islands | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>2160 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Lagombi ( **Target**)<br>Aknosom<br>Great Baggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Lagombi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This beast bears some similar mannerisms to the bear-like Arzuros, but can slide quickly across the Frost Islands to attack, reposition and retreat. On that note, there are two types of attacking slide with one being a linear lunge that starts with the beast hunching over and a circular slide that follows it hunching in a slight coil in its posture. The defensive slides do some damage, but cannot launch you into the air so it is mainly important to recognise that a slide could be followed by a small lunge. As it has sensitive hearing, it is especially vulnerable to Sonic Bombs when sliding and will even tumble as a result, you should keep in mind that these won't be part of the Supply Box and how it won't be affected once it is enraged.

Other attacks to consider include its habit of throwing snowballs like the high snowball which can catch you out of the air which leaves you in Iceblight (which slows down the Wirebug gauge recovery - recover by rolling or using a Nulberry) and leaves a pile of snow that can slow down your movement. Other snowball attacks include a large snowball that only goes in front and throwing two balls to the sides, you can take the first one as a sign to reposition.

When the monster stands on its hind legs, it is a tell that it may transition to a series of actions like claw swipes but also has an attack to account for threats behind which follows it wriggling its rear. One last attack to consider is its jumping attack where the landing impact will make you lose balance even if it misses, it can be read from it hunching over and wriggling its whole body while facing a target but mind that the roll that it does after it lands is an attack as well. The target monster will leave to feed near Area 5 and go to sleep in Area 6 when tuckered out, but if you get the aid of the Great Baggi's Mounted Punisher you may be able to force it to nap early.

* * *

| **Wroggi Place, Wroggi Time** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>2880 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Great Wroggi ( **Target**)<br>Aknosom<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Wroggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The Great Wroggi is similar to Baggi in colder climates as it can fire a projectile to weaken prey and threats alike, but this time it is poison whether through a cloudy mist or spit that travels in an arc. In both cases, it tends to back away which can be considered an additional tell of its intent but two poison attacks are quite distinct as the spit goes in an arc which can easily miss nearby hunters (not a problem for the smaller Wroggi) and the cloud is unblockable while covering a lot of ground in front of it.

A common habit is for it to strike with its charging tackle right almost immediately after the poison cloud after aligning itself horizontally, which can chip away at your recoverable health. It is best to avoid being in the wrong place and the wrong time by circling the monster so you don't have to use too many Antidotes from the Supply Box or Maido at the Guild Store. Alternatively, you can heal yourself through the poison as it will wear off with time or seek out an Antidobra in advance from the top of the falls between Area 6 and 2. When tired, it won't be able to hurl any poison but it can still throw its weight around and you'll be able to find it sleeping in Area 6.

* * *

| **A Grizzly Encounter** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>2160 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Arzuros ( **Target**)<br>Great Izuchi<br>Great Wroggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Arzuros**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

You may already be familiar from seeing it thrash about during the Training Missions from Hinoa's Quest Counter or think you've seen it all everything if you've hunted down the Lagombi already. However, this bear-like beast's all-fours charging tackle is a run rather than a slide which allows it to pivot and limit the room to evade in linear spots like the path between Areas 7 and 8. Similar to the bunny beast, it can repeat the tackle multiple times but can actually lock you down in a corner making haphazard Wirefalls a greater risk.

It also has a range of attacks from the bipedal stance with the most unique one being its "honey stealing throw". As the name suggests, the monster will grab you when tired and steal your honey to restore itself on the spot. It is important to have a Wirebug spare to Wirefall so you can get on the counter-attack quickly or set up traps.

* * *

| **Off the Beaten Path** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>1080 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Arzuros<br>Great Izuchi<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Altaroth **(Target)**, Bnahabra, Izuchi **(Target)**, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 20 Izuchi or Altaroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

As a slaying quest, the larger wild monsters aren't of interest but only the smaller Altaroth and Izuchi that are roaming the mountains but that doesn't mean that you shouldn't either prepare just in case you cross paths with one, have the intent to collect some drops or want to capture/slay them.

Since the Great Izuchi is hard to find without two of its best warriors, it may be a good call to pursue that monster while taking the Poison Smoke Bomb from the Supply Box as a precaution.

* * *

| **Hot Topic Hooligans** |
| --- |
| Rank: **1★** | Locale:<br>Frost Islands | Locale Info:<br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money:<br>1080 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Great Baggi<br>Great Izuchi<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra ( **Target**), Baggi, Zamite ( **Target**), Izuchi |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 20 Zamite or Bnahabra**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

A simple slaying quest though both of the targeted smaller monsters can cause ailments if you let them make their mark. The Zamite can actually grow after **draining** you while the Bnahabra can **paralyse** you with their stinger, it is fairly straightforward to avoid or block these approaches. In the case, that you either need to get the Zamite off you or need more reach to catch the winged Bnahabra, be sure to use the Throwing Kunai.

In a pitch, you can throw off the Zamite by either rolling multiple times or using Wiredash but it will grow and mature even from one feeding even if it doesn't become Zamtrios right away. You can take this as a chance to give it some literal growing pains after it leeches off you.

* * *

| **Dead Ringer** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3960 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Tetranadon **(Target)**<br>Arzuros<br>Great Izuchi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tetranadon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This toadversary is more just than the spitting image of the mechanical dummies in the Training Area and Buddy Plaza. Starting on all-fours, it tackles threats with the ability to leap into the sky and effectively Sumo Slam the earth into immobilising you even if it doesn't hit the mark. The palm strikes and charging bite attack are bog water standard but one move that sets it apart is a Clap (which follows raising forelegs to both sides) since it can stun you on close impact, A second unique attack is a lunging munch attack where it attempts to grab a hunter and swallow them whole along with the dirt they stood on. The aftermath of the latter can be escaped quickly with Wirefall, so make sure you keep a Wirebug spare if possible.

Whether it decides to eat the earth alone or does so with you included, it will eventually bloat up to the point that it can no longer do some actions but it does raise its head in the new bipedal stance. This may make maximising damage or stun more difficult even if it is a bigger target. In this state, it has a Sumo Stomp which shatters the stones to whichever side it raised and lowered its hind leg onto (huge opening as it recovers) and can dig into the ground again to hurl a boulder to throw at you. While other attacks are safe enough to block, you are likely to be waterlogged by the boulder (Use Nulberries from the supply box or Wiredash to shake off the Waterblight). There is also a water vapour tossing attack after reeling back an arm which can force a stumble even from afar from it is off-target.

It may seem like a good call to lead it away from water though you'll still need to consider a water beam attack (telegraphed by stepping back and gargling) that goes forward, sweeps across then finally vertically that can be blocked. It is also able to fire a powerful water bullet regardless of the form, so you can't fight from a distance without being on the move. Stay agile and you'll be able to make this frog croak and bite the dust for good.

* * *

| **I Want Off This Ride** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2** ★ | Locale:<br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5400 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Bishaten **(Target)**<br>Great Wroggi<br>Royal Ludroth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Bishaten**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Make sure to pick the Antidotes from the Supply Box as not only the target monster but a Wild Monster can inflict poison. When you find the Bishaten, you will likely find it on all fours but it won't be long before it is pivoting on its tail, hind legs and forelegs with each stance having its own benefits and risks which even play into how it throws projectiles.

This monster prefers to leap around its prey as if it were a Capoeira dancer, so it only has a few claw swipes where it attacks from in place with other attacks on all-fours including a flip to strike with its tail which leaves it stuck for a while and a wild attack that follows doing a flip backwards. It much prefers to leap away while throwing Jumbofruit (yellow, no ailment) and Poisonfruit (purple, inflicts poison on hit) from its pouch. Thanks to its prehensile tail, it can also perform additional three fruit attacks with the Jumbo one having greater range, the Poison leaving toxic mist and a Flashfruit (flashing yellow) attack blinding anyone else in the area. Mind that all three have clear tells as the fruit unique appearances and the monster treats them differently, even covering its mouth after the Poisonfruit tail attack and its eyes before and during the Flashfruit attack.

Once on its forelegs, the head is easier to reach but this stance allows it to perform the flipping tail attack multiple times with very little recovery but no ability to correct direction till it stops. From standing on its tail, there are actually two subtle stances with one allowing it to move around by spinning which can lead into a spinning top charge after it coils up and to perform a flipping tail strike rapidly but with a bit of a pause to recover which allows it to turn to chase a target. There is also a stationary tail stand which can be told apart by how its tail coils away from its body to hold the ground with the end rather than coiling inward. This stationary tail stance allows it to throw fruit with both hands-free which it may do rapidly if it isn't swaying to strike nearby threats with its wings.

If you want to get the biggest advantage, you may want to use the Mounted Punisher of the Royal Ludroth (Waterblight, weakens hide) and Great Wroggi (Poison, self-explanatory) and to keep the pressure on when it balances on its tail in place. If you knock the monster down, it may drop a handful of fruit (Poisonfruit - thrown poison projective, Jumbofruit - heals you on use, Flashfruit - thrown blinding projectile) which you can continue to use on the hunt. Mind that these items are too dangerous to keep after a hunt, so it is important to use them while you can.

* * *

| **Way of the Pukei** |
| --- |
| Rank: 2★ | Locale:<br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5400 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Pukei-Pukei **(Target)**<br>Barroth<br>Volvidon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Pukei-Pukei**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Be sure to take some **antidotes** from the Supply Box as the target wyvern is toxic from back to front. This winged wyvern does have telltale signs of when it will use poison attacks which you can even see from how its mouth and tail may be swollen after a meal, this is a sign that poisonous spit may spread, linger or rise up as a persistent gas. This gives more layers to anticipate how the battle may go if you recognise where and what the herbs or mushrooms are in the area, perhaps setting a trap to call out it out after it looks around and slobbers with its great tongue.

On that note, the tongue is one of its greatest assets for extending its range but it is especially sensitive to damage and happens to be tied to attacks with higher recovery even after retracting. It is still able to use poison spit attacks while flapping its wings back, while in place like a cannon and around it in general. Keep an eye on its tail since it can shoot long streams of poison gas both across and skyward that can launch hunters while inflicting an ailment, but if the end is severed, the range for physical and poison attacks will be severely severed.

* * *

| **The Flooding Flooded Forest** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4320 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Royal Ludroth **(Target)**<br>Arzuros<br>Rathian | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Royal Ludroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Get as many Nulberries as you need from the Supply Box to counteract being thrashed and splashed by the Royal Ludroth and even the common Droths. While the target monster's greatest strengths lie in its mass and mastery of water, you can work to reduce both by cutting its tail and by leading it away from water areas though it will likely retreat to restore itself in Area 6 near a Waterfall.

It puts both water and weight to good use when enraged as it can charge across the area multiple times while firing water blasts, however, it also boasts water shots that get extended range if you allow it to fight in its element. Its size can prove as a weakness for it as it has difficulty with pivoting to attack threats to its side or behind it besides rolling to its side or a tail whip. It is also notable how its mane is actually more vulnerable to sharp and electric damage than its head since it is a spongy hide for retaining water. Regardless, you'll have to be aware of how it does have the ability to pivot with specific attacks with one last highlight being a spinning tail strike that follows it raising its head while shooting water to turn around.

* * *

| **So Muddy Hardheaded!** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5400 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Barroth **(Target)**<br>Basarios<br>Rathian | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Barroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

It is fairly typical for a monster's head to be the most desirable target even if being face to face exclusively is poor form, however, this bullish beast muddies the waters. The brute's head happens to be its most armoured spot of all, both taking less damage and bouncing back many a melee attack unless you aim to stun it. If you would like the batter the Barroth, you need to consider its tail (which can be severed) and torso, though its forelegs and hind legs are also potential targets.

The monster isn't just headstrong literally, but can also increase the range of many of its head, tail and body attacks after it stores mud by rolling around in wet patches in the Sandy Plains. While most moves fling mud bullets (which either cause Waterblight on impact - take the Supply Nulberries - or leaves muck after landing), two notable attacks don't get affected by this at all. One is a bull-like charge which can collide into walls stopping it short and a second is when it shakes off mud to its sides which can make it difficult to fight from mid-range but is an opening if you are adept from afar, happen to be close or have a gap-closing method to get some damage.

* * *

| **A Pale Shadow** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3960 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Khezu **(Target)**<br>Great Baggi<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Khezu**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Khezu may be a shock as it doesn't have music that plays as you battle it, word is that monsters only have a theme after they see you or your companions so you'll have to contend with a chorus of roars. On one hand, its lack of sight may give an opening to set up traps, but on the other, the monster has a habit of using massive roars to disrupt threats which gives it a chance to orient and sense you. There are means to get around the roar with well-timed defensive actions or even interrupt the monster as it lowers its head for the screech, but you will most likely be interrupted if you don't pace your approach.

The monster's physiology is fairly standard with the head and neck taking the most damage, but the tail is especially tough as it needs to be strong to help the monster root itself to the ground, ceilings and walls. On that note, you can tell apart its electrical attacks from the behaviour of its tail as it is rooted to the ground before shooting blasts forward which are conducted along and up surfaces much like Pikachu's Thunder Jolt in Smash Bros. When the tail isn't rooted while standing on the ground, Khezu shoots a projectile into the air that arcs before landing with an explosion. This move is also done from the ceiling if the monster gets the chance to fight while in a cave, but you can use Wiredash or long-ranged attacks to knock it down when it is hanging down.

It is best to be careful as it can switch to leaping from the ceiling to attack with an electric tackle (also done on the ground) with all of its electric moves including Thunderblight (cure with Supply Box's Nulberry). In essence, defeating the Khezu is about managing your distance whether that is done through standard-issue Throwing Kunai, Wiredash actions or using slopes to allow melee attacks and resheathing to become safer. Khezu can seem impossible to reach when it gets in the habit of docking its tail below it to zap anything close like Street Fighter's Blanka but also overextends with its neck at other times which can be a big opening.

* * *

| **Reinventing the Wheel** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4320 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Volvidon **(Target)**<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku<br>Pukei-Pukei | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Volvidon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This hunt revolves around following Volvidon's habits as it effectively doesn't have a "weak side" that it can't reach on short notice, unlike many other monsters. It can also cause **two nasty ailments** whether you are in front of it or around it. The first ailment comes from the Stench that it scatters around it while on all-fours with a brown gas in all directions but towards its face, it is so rancid that any afflicted hunter needs to use **Deodorant** (supplied by the guild in the box for this quest) before they can stomach any consumable items - mind that it is blockable if need be. The second ailment is **paralysis from the toxin that spits out while bipedal, there is no cure for this** if it connects properly but an ally can launch you which can get you out of a pitch and shake it off.

The monster also has range thanks to its long tongue to snapping at threats in front but also has burst options as it can often coiling into a sphere to roll backwards, forward, sideways and even drift at times. Generally, it is difficult to punish the ball beast with melee while it is using the rolling attacks, but one of them has it leap into the sky before landing on its back. This is a rare case where you can move in as it unfurls forward to all-fours for the head, the weakest part of the monster though it is worth noting that the back (the zigzagging part) and front upper half of the monster are also good targets to maximise impact. In a pitch, you can tack on damage with standard-issue kunai if it is bouncing around and other tactics can't be interrupted by a roar, at least not by this monster.

* * *

| **Blasted Basarios!** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5400 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Basarios **(Target)**<br>Barroth,<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Basarios**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The gas-blasting Basarios can be tough to tackle both due to its heavy plating and due to its ability to spew both poisonous gas and flames from its body. Fortunately, you can have some Supply Box items to address this with the explosive EZ Large Barrel Bomb to break up the shell on its abdomen and Antidote for poison, but the Fireblight may have to be addressed by running, dropping and rolling. That being said, most of the monster is clad in stone which leaves the tail tip, head, legs and abdomen as the few safe spots to strike with regular melee attacks - also giving more damage.

Generally, the monster has linear and obvious attacks but ones that can cover a lot of distance so be sure to read when it will roll out of a run which leaves its head exposed low to the ground, it also does the roll from neutral following raising its head. It may also skid out of a run, but you usually have enough time to recognise its chosen attack option including its gas-based attacks with one interesting one being when it steps forward with flaming gas. In the case that you can't get a good angle to strike, it may be better to focus on long-range to tack on damage, though you have to be aware that while it isn't the most mobile monster that it can actually pivot when it digs into the ground and can strike with its tail while walking backwards.

If you want a better chance to break through its shell, you may want to take control of a Barroth or simply take advantage of a turf war as it can inflict Waterblight on your target.

* * *

| **The Path to Royalty** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5400 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Rathian **(Target)**<br>Bishaten<br>Pukei-Pukei | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rathian**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While your target may be known as the "Queen of the Land", this royal wyvern can also roam through the skies for a moment as it either attacks or switches to a flying stance, both allow the Rathian to inflict poison in a flash with a tail flip with it being the primary attack from the air with the second attack being it flying into a faraway threat. While this provides an opening as it has to land slowly to recover, the monster is able to flip multiple times without landing when enraged (see the flames from its mouth). In the case, you are hit you can count on an antidote from the Supply Box.

On that note, a second helpful resource from the Supply Box is EZ Meat Bait which can be used to put the Flooded Forest's Giganha in a frenzy that chip away at the target monster if it gets too close. This can be done in areas (for example Area 4) that the Rathian will go to eat a Slagtoth when injured, which may be a good opportunity to go for the head, wings and tail (which can be severed to shorten the Tail Flip's range). Other attacks of interest include its fire breath which is fairly standard between a single fireball, triple fireballs and a high impact fireball and its running tackle that can be repeated many times when enraged.

* * *

| **Dawn of the Kestodon** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1440 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Basarios<br>Pukei-Pukei<br>Volvidon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon **(Target)**, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay 20 Kestodon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While the mission description asks for twenty, you **only have to get rid of fifteen when you get on the field**. These headstrong herbivores can be told apart by their physique, colouring and head shape besides the larger males being brasher and keen to charge into any threats. While it may be safer to slay them from a distance, you can either block or avoid their rushing tackles to get an opening if you don't use counter moves for a counterattack. Mind that the front of the head may bounce back a blunt approach though.

* * *

| **Hide, Hide, Hide!** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>1800 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Arzuros<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku<br>Royal Ludroth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Remobra, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth **(Target)**, Slagroth, Jagras **(Target)** |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 20 Jagras or Ludroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

For this task, you just need to seek out and slain any count of the smaller monsters named that adds up to twenty. While the spikey Jagras are fairly isolated and don't cause any ailments, the lesser but still leviathan Ludroth can inflict Waterblight while mostly settling in watery areas that their king will path through on occasion. If you are having trouble with the hordes, the Supply Box has two items that are especially helpful in the Ludroth's domain, EZ Meat Bait for shoals of Giganha and Nulberry in case of blight.

* * *

| **Bouncing Brawlers** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5400 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br> Lagombi **(Target)**<br>Tetranadon **(Target)**<br>Khezu | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Lagombi and a Tetranadon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The Supply Box has some Nulberries which can help with dealing with the usual ailments as well as EZ Screamer Pods can be used to disrupt the monsters on the move that have sensitive hearing. If you are having difficulty with stopping the bouncing brawlers, it is worth considering bringing traps to keep them still and capture them as that is still classified as a hunt (even with different drops). You can also get the bunny and kappa duo to battle each other whether via Stinkmink luring them together or by wyvern riding either one to battle the other.

* * *

| **Fried and Baptized** |
| --- |
| Rank: **2★** | Locale:<br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>6840 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Royal Ludroth **(Target)**<br>Rathian **(Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this ENDURANCE quest is to **hunt a Royal Ludroth and a Rathian**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Unlike hunts, where the wild monsters are always on the prowl, endurance quests have the additional targets appear later on which gives you a chance to work with one at a time, but also cuts down on your time to fight later targets especially if you count on luring or riding a monster to battle each other.

You do have a lot of resources to work with from the Supply Box (EZ Meat Bait, Antidote, EZ Flash Bomb, Nulberry), but you want to guarantee a better chance against two regal wyverns if may be worth considering bringing traps and tranq items. That being said, it is still possible to ride one monster into another's domain to bring an end to their royal rivalry.

* * *

| **Hellfire** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale:<br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8640 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Magnamalo (Target)**<br>Rathian<br>Volvidon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt Magnamalo**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Between the cold blues and flaming reds of the Lava Caverns, no area poses an increased danger to the Devourer of the Rampage though the burning ground can drain your health if you stand on your own two feet without a Palamute to tread lightly. Otherwise, you can have to decide where to clash with the beast based on how you can exploit its hunger habits (Area 5 on Rhenoplos) for example and should take as much Deodorant (Supply Box) as you need as a backup plan in case you are short on Wirebugs when Hellfireblighted. You may want to consider a plan for your Palamute as it can't give you a ride while blighted and since Magnamalo can even roar into performing a second one with explosive force.

If you want to cover all your bases against the cover monster, the head is the weakest part if you are using either sharp or blunt melee approaches, though the Tailblade (the tip not the whole tail) and Armblades (golden parts on the front legs) take the most damage from projectile attacks while also taking significant damage from swords or hammers. That being said, you may have trouble if your chosen melee attacks do not ignore sharpness or if you don't soften its tough parts with Waterblight (which cannot be done with wyvern rideable monsters here). Water and Thunder are its greatest weaknesses so it may pay off to take advantage of this.

The Magnamalo does have a second form with brighter magenta flames (Magentamalo? Whatever we call it there is yet another form with its spikes raised) though it is important to focus on how it distributes its flames first and foremost. When you see the flames on its Tailblade means that its stinger attack will leave hellfire vapour which will explode after a while and them being on its arms mean that it striking the earth in front of it will also make it explode. This makes it a better idea to retreat directly away after it lines its head and tail together for the Stinger if it hasn't gotten the hellfire charge on the tail, but it is still generally better to circle around the beast so you can get better chances to strike weak points. On the note of the tail, your target's intent can be told from how it is positioned since it whirls its tail before the Hellfire Cannon. Other examples include how the monster will usually wait till it has flicked its tail following any moves which leave vapour (note the exceptions like the next paragraph) and how the arcs for hellfire balls can't cover near opponents if you can slip by while it is walking or standing.

Magnamalo's magnum opus has it flip backwards, roar with vapour seeping around it before tackling into the player to launch them before divebombing with all of its might, though it can also perform the divebomb alone. It can be difficult to time a dive-evade (commonly called the "Superman Dive", a weaponless running evade away from a large monster - it has full invincibility) for the full attack, but there is also the option to block as long as you have the stamina and health to take it. In the worst-case scenario, you can use a Wirefall before the divebomb part when launched though you have to time it properly with the correct direction. A more limited alternative is you can use Flash Bombs to disrupt the monster which can create a big opening depending on when it catches the Wynern of Malice.

* * *

| **Beckoning Slumber** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>6480 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Somnacanth (Target)**<br>Almudron<br>Tobi-Kadachi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Somnacanth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

On your hunt for the shell-obsessed Somnacanth, the Supply Box has three items that may come in handy with one being EZ Meat Bait to use against water-dwelling foes near Giganha and another being EZ Screamer Pod that can stop its sleep mist ring. The third item (Energy Drink) is more focused on reversing the sleep ailment that it can inflict with mist attacks. Since **all three monsters on the prowl boast powerful immobilising roars**, it may be best to isolate the target especially away from water if possible unless you can guarantee that they will be fighting for you and not against you.

More specifically on how to take Somnacanth down, the weakest points to strike are its Neck, Head (not the barbed Head Fin) and Tail and you have to acknowledge how it exposes these points as it moves into different stances and attacks. Firstly, the front stroke stance allows it to cut through the water for a quick tackle, short-range talon swipes and a sleep-inducing mist beam that goes forward. Its backstroke stance allows it to pivot around the area with its forelegs free to use shell-based attacks to inflict blind and Blastblight (evade or use Deodorant to clear) but also to spurt out sleep-inducing mist on the go which can make it easier to strike with other attacks.

The last stance is "tailstand" which still allows it to pivot to get around while extending the reach of strikes with its talons greatly. Your biggest opening to counterattack is after its head fin sweep (follows it raising its head) or during its mist attacks though both of them cover a lot more surface area in this stance. One mist attack sweeps the area in front of it of the leviathan but another starts with its neck emitting mist as it raises its head before mist sweeps the area in all directions. While the monster is more skilled in inflicting ailments than brutal force, you shouldn't overlook how it can leap from the tailstand into the air to tackle a foe while also landing Waterblight.

* * *

| **The Blizzard Blender** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>9360 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Goss Harag (Target)**<br>Barioth<br>Tigrex | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/55/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/53/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/60/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Goss Harag**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Goss Harag is a cold-blooded beast that can equally throw its mass into any prey, chill it to the bone or do both at the same time. It may be weak to fire, but this hunt does not have any wild monsters nor free Supply Box items (only Nulberry for Iceblight) that you can use to take advantage of this, so you'll have to focus on the key weak points (Ice Clump, Head and Forelegs). The furry monster is highly mobile, so you will have to be cautious of when it jumps into the air to slam the ground as it creates a forward column of rubble that can launch a hunter, but it is also an attack that Goss Harag recovers from quickly enough to fire an ice beam follow up whenever it is enraged.

This ice beam will go vertically up before across before the monster switches its tactics by forming either one or two ice blades (called Ice Clumps in your Hunter's Notes). This extends the reach of its claw swipes and automatically sends projectiles that cause Iceblight, changes around a bipedal running punch for it slamming or stabbing the ground (multiple times when two ice blades are present - it recovers quickly from the stab so retreat if that's the case). Fortunately, the red flaring parts when enraged are actually weak points and the weakest of all are the ice clumps on its forelegs due to how they are fused with its body. You can break them to down the monster for a big opening but will have to slip through the frost and stone that it digs up to do so. Two attacks that give particularly big openings are when it recovers from the running punch without any ice blades (distinct as it runs on two legs with one hand behind) and when it does five swipes with its double ice blades when it returns to normal, but you may be able to take advantage of it eating (Anteka) and sleeping in Area 6 as well.

* * *

| **Zigzagging Zapper** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>6480 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Tobi-Kadachi (Target)**<br>Bishaten<br>Rathalos | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/58/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tobi-Kadachi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While no part of the jumpy Tobi-Kadachi is clearly clad in scales, the weakest point to strike is the large tail though its head, neck and torso and back are fine alternatives. It has highly mobile but very vulnerable to ailments with two examples including poison (use the Poison Kunai from the Supply Box, or a Poisonfrog if you find one) and Waterblight which prevents it from being able to charge up into a second form.

After Tobi-Kadachi jumpstarts itself into its charged form (it happens after raising its tail), it is able to toss spikes after spinning and even bite while inflicting Thunderblight besides gaining the ability to glide in the air to do two attacks in the air after going forward. One attack is linear; a tail flip attack that only strikes ahead of it but it can also whip to both sides which can be told apart by how its tail bends to the side or alternatively how the head reaches the ground first before the whip happens.
