It is better to punish the aerial spin attack.

When Tobi-Kadachi decides to hang on a wall to catch a breather, it doesn't have a lot of options from there if it hasn't charged up already and you should find a way to set a trap, strike it in the air or use projectiles if you don't have a Flash Bomb.

* * *

| **Down 'n' Dirty** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>9360 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Almudron (Target)**<br>Anjanath<br>Diablos | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/54/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Almudron**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

As you get down in the dirt, you should take note that the monster's weak points are its tail tip (to sharp damage) and its head (to blunt and gun attacks) though the tail is a great target too. Almudron has two general stances to consider, one standing with its tail behind and another where it is swimming with its abdomen submerged in the earth and tail in front. The monster is able to move with relative ease, but mainly when it dives out of sight which gives you a bit of time to retreat if you anticipate something big.

Since "mud" is in the middle of its name, it is no surprise that it even causes mud platforms to appear passively which can both hinder a keen hunter but also give new aerial approaches. While on all fours, the monster can flick mud in aggressive formations including muck bullets forward but also sweeping around it to make a wave that causes Waterblight (Wiredash to shake it off) but also leaves muck as it settles. It can also strike with great force with its tail with it is with a stronger range punch after coiling its body or after turning and raising its hind legs to slam on anything in the way. Almudron's tail also gives it the ability to pivot and reach far when striking with its forelegs, though it is pretty telegraphed as it is the only case that has the monster raises the front half of its body.

The swimming stance is another story that starts with it dipping into the mud but always slamming its tail down. Mind that **the monster only slams its tail down from the initial dive**, not following it leaping out of the mud into another part of the area and that both swimming and standing stances leave the weakest points exposed. If you manage to enrage the monster, its body will change colour, attack faster and the mud in many attacks will turn gold. Not all that glitters is golden as the muck will burn any prey that touches it, so it becomes especially important to have options to escape upwards and away. The **angry acidic Almudron** will also change from doing the double foreleg strike to doing a foreleg strike followed by a tail whip that sends muck.

During the battle, you may see it spinning around before diving into the mud to form a boulder that attaches to its tail with mucus; it does stop the monster from being able to move as freely and removes the mud flicking but also makes an impact by making the standing tail punch more of a wrecking boulder. Mind that the monster can and will leap into the air to slam a threat with Waterblight, even though its backside becomes an even larger weak point from a distance. If you stay on its tail to the end, it may make its own domain of muck in Area 7 with massive columns of mud to blocking easy approaches with a habit of slapping away threats while submerged. It is key to recognise that the monster is usually in danger as it recovers out of the mud, but it can also flip out of it while enraged.

* * *

| **Bully of the Caverns** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7560 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Anjanath (Target)**<br>Royal Ludroth<br>Tigrex | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/60/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Anjanath**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

To make the brute wyvern a bullied wyvern, you may want to force them to fight someone their own size like the Royal Ludroth as it boasts powerful water attacks and can soften the monster up with Waterblight. Ultimately, you'll have the size up with the Anjanath directly and find a way to reach its weak points (head, snout, wings and tail) since they only end up being close to the ground for brief windows. You'll want to recognise its habits by watching its head since it usually bites once after turning but can bite three times while gaining ground (five when enraged) which makes it predictable. When Anjanath raises its head while running forward, it is trying to increase torque as it swings its head and often oversteps the target with the wide attack, though a lowered stance with the top of its skull facing you means it will charge with all its might.

Generally, your target has a bit of a naff time with turning but it will be resourceful enough to try to flick at foes at its heels and to whip any foes directly behind it and far away though it can leap a great distance while causing tremors and toss a rock with its jaws. Things do change when its snout becomes enflamed since it can breathe a beam of fire after backing away slightly (prevent this by striking its neck to stop the organ that glows through its flesh), can 180-degree tail whip while causing a gust that makes nearby hunters stumble and do a wild double bite charge attack. This last attack can have the monster turn slightly between the bites and juggle its prey in such a way that it can catch bad Wirefalls, so be sure to circle around the monster if you see it lower its head with the jaws open.

* * *

| **Twilit Twin Stars** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7560 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Nargacuga (Target)**<br>Magnamalo<br>Rathalos | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/57/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/58/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Nargacuga**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The lean, mean Nargacuga doesn't specialise in inflicting ailments, however, it is pretty tricky to down due to its excellent reach from all sides. The weak point is its head (sharp and blunt), but you'll have to keep an eye on its tail (the tip can be severed - to shorten range) as well as its "Cutwings" that are tough, deflective parts connected to the forearms though they are especially frail against fire and electric attacks.

In addition to catlike leaps around and directly onto prey, the jet-black beast is renowned for its tail strikes as it can crouch down to ready a tail whip that can cross a surprising range but it also has a spinning tail attack which usually follows it raising its head with its mouth open. The Cutwing attacks are also key to recognise as the monster recovers from them quicker in addition to them being tough to break, but you can throw off Narga's balance if you follow through. When Nargacuga is enraged with red glowing eyes, you can expect some new attacks like it rattling its tail before sending spikes your way and a sneaky tail slam that follows it walking slowly with its body to the ground.

If you need more firepower, you can use the EZ Screamer Pod against its sensitive hearing or count on the fiery Rathalos to Sky-High Dive in or perhaps the hell-fiery Magnamalo to divebomb in as an unlikely ally against the nimble Nargacuga.

* * *

| **My Muse the Mizutsune** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8280 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Mizutsune (Target)**<br>Somnacanth<br>Tobi-Kadachi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Mizutsune**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The fox wyvern regularly sends a wave of bubbles which makes it harder to read where you position yourself, especially as the white bubbles cause Bubbleblight that cause the afflicted party to slip and slide (use a Cleanser from the supply box or roll around to recover). While there are also positive bubbles (attack-boosting red & healing green) and you can even burst bubbles in flight with kunai, you shouldn't lose sight of the main threat.

As for catching the bubble beast, Mizutsune can be a slippery one though you can take note of its tells before major attacks like how it raises its claw while on its hind legs before a claw strike with great reach, but notable recovery. Things do change if you let the monster get angry and spin in a circle to lather up while releasing bubbles that orbit around it. Following this, the monster will be able to glide across the area with ease even while spinning with a pressured water beam (escape to higher ground or Wiredash above) but also be able to skate around to claw at you as you are avoiding or pressuring bubbles from even more angles than before.

You can take advantage of its habit of always being around water by using EZ Meat Bait to interrupt its flow, but may want to consider getting other wild monsters as Somnacanth can put it to sleep with its Mounted Punisher while Tobi-Kadachi inflicts Thunderblight which can make it easier to stun and stop the bubble hell. On that note, the weak points are the head, dorsal fin, neck, tail and hind leg in that order with the forelegs and claws being tougher but especially sensitive to electric or ice damage.

* * *

| **Champion of the Caverns** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8280 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Rathalos (Target)**<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku<br>Magnamalo | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/58/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rathalos**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Much like Rathian, your target's emerald counterpart of the land, Rathalos has two stances with one being on the ground and the other soaring the skies. While it prefers to fly, it is worth noting its key attacks from the ground as it has some unique habits like a nasty double bite from standing which can easily catch bad Wirefalls (also inflicts Fireblight when enraged) but it can also flap its wings to hop in place for a small stomp or flap away a bit to shoot a fireball. Generally, it is best to keep the monster on the ground since the double tail whip and most fire attacks are predictable, but when it flaps into the air especially when enraged with flames seeping from its mouth things change.

From the air, Rathalos can fly by and shoot frying fireballs before gliding to attack and land, strike with its tail to whip to the side before landing, shoot three fireballs before landing, dive kick from across the area (inflicts poison - use the supply Antidote), glide kick before doing other attacks and more. One key thing to consider most attacks from the air and the ground have the monster facing towards its prey, this makes the supply EZ Flash Bomb effective at grounding it immediately for a large opening.

As far as the continued effectiveness of the Flash Bomb, when you come at the King of the Skies, you best not miss since Rathalos eventually builds up resistance to the point it only makes him pause and need to land down slowly which can be an opportunity in itself if you have traps, Flashbugs or Flash Bombs. This is a champion time to strike weak points like the head, tail (can be severed) and tail especially with electric or water attacks.

* * *

| **Wavering Moon and Thunder** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8280 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Zinogre (Target)**<br>Nargacuga<br>Rathian | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/62/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/57/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Zinogre**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The proud Zinogre can be hard to pin down as it dances during battles between firing electric projectiles and faking out before charging up to reach another form which is mixed faster and slower attacks. This is all the more reason to be a good dance partner by following the tells for when you can attack safely. Some key examples in its normal state have it charge electricity from a step (recognise the sparks before the foreleg lands) or backstep spin for a small opening, though you can get more if you catch the monster after a spinning tail strike (note the tail over its head and backstep) or tail flip.

As far as weak points, you should mainly be focusing on the head, back, hind leg and hip with ice, water and fire elements (Rathian can help during a turf war or wyvern ride) if possible. While Zinogre's tough tail tip can be a problem, you can sever it off if you cut close to the base. If you do this, you may have less to worry about when it goes into its **Supercharged State (directly out of a charge with a hitbox - stay back)** though it will still be able to inflict Thunderblight with the step attacks from before (use Nulberries from the supply box to recover).

While Zinogre is more fierce in its final form with a new attack pattern, it is key to note that prior attacks are still present with similar punishes being relevant. Additionally, you can knock it back to normal with enough pressure. From there, it may be desperate to recharge and backspin to get an opening to do so towards the end of a fight. If you have trouble keeping up, you can use **EZ Meat Bait** (supply box) in the watery areas to disrupt its rhythm.

* * *

| **White Knight vs. Hunter** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>6480 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Barioth (Target)**<br>Goss Harag<br>Mizutsune | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/53/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/55/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Barioth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Barioth may seem bulky and ill-suited to the climate at a glance, but actually has an easy time clinging onto and reorienting on the ice in the area thanks to its winged forelegs. Be sure to break them to slow down the monster for an easier fight though the head is a weaker weak point for sharp, blunt and gun damage if you can only reach that. On a related note, its tail can be severed to take the icy wind out of Barioth's sails while being a weak point as well.

As far as its attacks, it can spit out an ice projectile that spawns an ice cyclone (use supply Nulberries for Iceblight) even if it is blocked (launches on hit) but is otherwise fairly linear with a short-range Hip Check and standard leaps. Like mentioned before, it is quite mobile due to its ice movement but it can also flap its wings to reposition or to escape backwards after being thrashed (try cornering it). You should expect to have to work for openings against this one which can be helped by waiting for it to eat in Area 6 for an ambush or by making an unlikely alliance with Goss Harag or Mizutsune.

When you eventually enrage the monster, you'll be able to tell from its quicker movements and red glowing eyes rather than blue as well as a powerful leap attack where it rams into the earth after stepping back and raising its head. This attack missing is one of the biggest openings you may get from the monster as it can flap into the air, though a well-placed Flash Bomb (not included as Supply) can change that. Stay frosty and you may be able to end the White Knight's Chronicles.

* * *

| **A Friend in Need** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>9360 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Tigrex (Target)**<br>Anjanath<br>Khezu | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/60/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tigrex**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The supply box has two items of interest that can slow down the fierce aggression of your target, one is Tinged Meat which can paralyse a hungry Tigrex but only once before it resists that side-effect entirely (though it eating is as an opening on face value whether in Area 5 or wherever you like). The EZ Flash Bomb works as you'd expect with the monster being dazed briefly before spinning in place or leaping imagined targets which makes it key to learn its tells and traits. First of all, its roar actually hurts if you are close to the body as it bellows so you should have an exit, defence or counter-attack plan when the beast spreads its forelegs or becomes enraged (clear from red veins and red eyes).

The cornerstone of Tigrex's attacks is its Wild Charge where it runs forward with its mouth open but can pivot to do it again, it may seem rudimental but the monster can mix up its approach by ending the second charge by stopping then backing off or by skidding then doing its iconic spinning tail attack. You can take advantage of the wild charge by leading it into breakable rocks and structures in the area where it will get stuck or by punishing the spinning tail strike since it is one of the highest recovery attacks that the monster has. On that note, it can spin without running which is one of its best attacks for clearing threats from all sides, be sure to recognise when its foreleg wings are pointing up with the tail swaying if you want to see it coming. Be sure to focus on the head, foreleg, tail base and tail (can be severed) especially with water or electric attacks (call in Khezu perhaps) if you want an easier time.

* * *

| **A Few Bumps Along the Way** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>9360 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Diablos (Target)**<br>Almudron<br>Barroth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/54/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Diablos**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While your target is a herbivore that mainly feeds on cacti in Area 9, you are in for a diabolical time if you treat it like the common Kelbi. Diablos's speciality is its ability to dig into the sand which makes it unreachable by normal attacks till it leaps out, if you use the supply box's EZ Screamer Pod (or Sonic Bombs that Rondine may have in her rare finds to sell) against its sensitive hearing - you will be able to force it out of the ground in a trapped-like state unless it is enraged (note the black mist near its mouth). In any case, the monster leaves a big opening to be attacked even when it isn't interrupted by high-frequency sounds, but you should remember that **its dig attack while enraged has high tracking as it leaps from the sand towards a target before doing a double horn lunge**.

Shifting to its attacks on the ground, Diablos can actually do the **double horn lunge** regardless of whether it is angry which maintains the same tracking between hits that catches poor Wirefalls that aren't high enough to escape. You can tell that the monster will do this from how it steps back and winds back its head. Another horn related duo of attacks to consider are its horn charge (ends with skidding) and horn launch (ends by raising head) where Diablos bends its wings and head down before charging forwards horns first. Both attacks are easy to avoid but it is even better if **you lead it into the ant hills** **so the hardheaded monster can be trapped into the quicksand pits**.

There are other attacks of note like the Hip Check which gets range extended by the tail, but you'll have less to worry about there if you focus on severing the tail (which affects the tail slam, tail double swing and double spin) as the club-like tailcase at the tip is massive. For specific places to hit, the abdomen, head, wing and tail are especially good particularly if you have water or ice attacks. The Mounted Punisher of Almudron can help reduce the need for sharpness through Waterblight as well since the horns and tail tip are pretty tough to crack.

* * *

| **Tail to Tail** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10080 z |
| Key | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Bishaten (Target)**<br>**Almudron (Target)**<br>Mizutsune | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Bishaten and an Almudron**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

It is up to you to tail whether to challenge the hand themed fighter or the whip one first, though you can put both of them against each other and the supply box has antidotes that can help you with the Bishaten in particular, but nothing for the Waterblight from Almudron or the roaming Mizutsune. It may be best to target Bishaten especially as it may drop Jumbofruit and Poisonfruit (you can hold as many as five of each) during the battle and even upon defeat which reduces the need to return to camp to resupply. That being said, it may be worth bringing some Flash Bombs for when it gets on walls to make the fight swifter.

When it comes to the Almudron, it is much the same as before but eats in a Bullfango in Area 9 and sleeps in Area 10 in the Shrine Ruins which makes it easy to stay on its tail.

* * *

| **Beastly Chaos** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10080 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Tigrex **(Target)**<br>Goss Harag **(Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/55/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/60/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this ENDURANCE quest is to **hunt a Tigrex and a Goss Harag**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While the deadly duo is more than willing to tear anything apart that interrupts their scraps, so you are fortunate that like other endurance quests that only have one target going berserk for a while. In this case, it is key to get big openings against Tigrex going by leading it into a rock when it charges.

The supply box also has some good resources to consider including Nulberries to deal with Waterblight and Iceblight and a flash bomb to bring a stop to the chaos. Mind that you can use the tinged meat (supply box) to get an opening on a hungry monster or go further by getting a full course of altered meat (from poison to sleep) from Rodine's store in exchange for some Kamura points.

* * *

| **Disastrously Beautiful** |
| --- |
| Rank: 3★ | Locale: <br>Arena | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10080 z |
| N/A | Conditions: None | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Barroth (Target)**<br>**Tobi-Kadachi (Target)**<br>**Magnamalo (Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) |
| Your goal in this ENDURANCE quest is to **hunt all target monsters**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

As an endurance quest in the arena, you can't count on a variety of endemic life or even a single Bombadgy to batter your opponents so you'll have to work with what you have. Before dropping in, there is a cornucopia of goodies in the Supply Box from EZ Farcasters to get back to resupply, Nulberries for all sorts of ailments, poison kunai (effective on all target monsters - but least effective on Magnamalo) and deodorant for Hellfireblight besides the usual inclusions. It is probably worth bringing your own Flash Bombs as they can give you a bit of room and interrupt monsters whether to stop the onslaught or just for a big opening.

From within the arena, you can call for the fence to be raised on either side (look for the Fence Switches - there are two for both sides of the fence) which can be beneficial for creating space (even Magnamalo's divebomb can be stopped) or forcing a matchup in a smaller zone. Mind that there is a cooldown with the fence not being an option for five minutes at the start and after it goes down. In any case, all three monsters have a different game plan with Barroth gradually getting weaker as it loses mud, Tobi-Kadachi tossing spikes or charging from a distance and Magnamalo is Magnamalo. Keep their flow in mind and you may able to play Poogie in the middle to make them interrupt each other's attacks and even inflict blights despite no interest in a turf war. Mind that they only take this kind of bait, not Meat Bait.

Lastly, don't give a thought to the audience and don't go down like a pit dog. Capturing the monsters during any quest with a "hunt" requirement is totally valid (no matter what some people say) especially as you can bring the materials to craft more traps if you need to capture three monsters or mess up with one of your traps.

* * *

| **The Swirling Gale** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3240 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Great Izuchi (Target)**<br>Aknosom<br>Great Wroggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Izuchi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Facing the Great Izuchi gang again especially in the old Shrine Ruins may seem like some sort of gag, but in High Rank every monster is especially sharp if you don't prepare and that goes triple when you are facing this classic trio's piercing claws and ripping tails. You can break up their team structure by using Flashes (Flashflies or Flash Bombs), or Stun for the less great among them. While the leader has a new wall leap attack if you fight the monster in the right area, you can handle it if you are a bit cautious or negate it by fighting in more open spaces.

Once weakened, you can count on using regular Raw Meat (either bring it yourself or carve - the Supply Box won't help here) to give an opening but you should be aware of its crescent moon tail slice which as it strikes around the Izuchi without being too high commitment. It always follows the monster forming a Crescent Moon pose as the name suggests.

* * *

| **Divine Comedy** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5760 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Arzuros (Target)**<br>**Kulu-Ya-Ku (Target)**<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Arzuros and Kulu-Ya-Ku**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This blue bear and red bird duo may not be allies, but they are inseparable and almost like twins in their hunger for things that don't belong to them. The third wheel, Tetranadon, may want to chew out hunters or rival large monsters if they cross its turf as well but you only have to worry about the targets especially as High Rank has new threats may appear as there is room for a new challenger (whether due to a hunt or others leaving the locale).

Starting with Kulu, it has the same familiar moveset though it may be quicker than the flock you've found before. Be sure to strike its high head or predict where the tail will be to crush its turning rock defence or use Flash Bombs/Flashflies to throw off its balance. As for the Arzuros, it has SIGNIFICANTLY more tracking on its running charge that can still correct after a skid so you can't count on casually jogging to avoid but you can make a fool of the bear if you lead it into the open Area 1 (Stinkmink or Wailnard can help) as you can circle around it more safely.

Some daring strategies to deal with the bear include using the local Bombadgy (Bombadgies?) as a projectile or deliberately getting caught with honey in your inventory. Arzuros has a hunger that can exceed self-preservation after it shakes you down which gives a big opening no matter where it eats or sleeps.

* * *

| **Didn't Get the Memo** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5400 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Great Baggi (Target)**<br>**Great Baggi (Target)**<br>Aknosom | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt 2 Great Baggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

You can't count on the Supply Box to stock you with Energy Drinks, but should be selective when or if you need to down even though the Great Baggi can now spit its sedative venom while jumping backwards (watch their mouths for the fluid as they turn) rather than being limited to an arc from a mid or long-range. This is as there are two targets that will likely call allies that will try to tranquilise you as you focus on the main threat's Hip Check or Neck Swing, but you aren't always at risk of carting every time.

On the note of allies, while the common Baggi will follow the greats, the Great Baggi brothers don't kneel to others and battle each other for dominance. These clashes give a great opportunity to put the winner to sleep if you can ride one and use their Mounted Punisher, but you should try to add Fire attacks for the most damage through Aknosom or your own means if that's your bag.

* * *

| **Dancing A-purr-ition** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4320 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Aknosom (Target)**<br>Arzuros<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Aknosom**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Aknosom has a lot of range between its wing swipes, beak stab and fireball attacks but you have a good chance if you recognise how it poses or even how it recovers in the air with select attacks like the Talon Flip (which starts with hopping on one leg). This is as the flashy umbrella bird is temporarily in flight and can be blinded with Flashflies or a Flash Bomb (bring your own) to make it drop to the ground, though it is generally safe to approach while airborne with its flying fireball attacks both in front and above it having poor reach below.

On the ground, you should be cautious about the striking from behind as it can kick up dirt for a mid-range attack as well as the embers (Fireblight) left by the Triple Fireball attack, while its combo where it picks and stomps on the ground can be troublesome due to the wind pressure and multiple patterns. Be sure to not overreach by being too close during this and keep an eye out for when it steps back before beginning a running charge since it often falls which gives a big opening but from a distance. The monster's showy nature is its weakness as its head, crest and massive wings are all especially weak to damage.

* * *

| **The Frost Islands Arena** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5040 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Tetranadon (Target)**<br>Great Baggi<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tetranadon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This high-rank toadversary keeps the two stances with one being on all-fours and the bloated bipedal form. You should expect it to move and recover faster as it can turn on its hind legs before leaping at a target (note its waving palms), but it is still fairly safe to approach the monster from its side or behind. This is as many of Tetranadon's actions take a long time for it to recover from regardless of form. For starters, its roar has great reach but only occurs after it finishes eating to become bipedal (this doesn't happen when the monster eats to recover from a thrashing in Area 7 doesn't make it grow, but is an opening still - similarly the roar doesn't occur after attempting to a hunter).

More aggressive examples include its standing stomp to the side (both sides when on all-fours), leaping stomp, rock throw while bipedal and running bipedal charge attack (though this can be followed by a water toss attack after the first charge). As long as you focus on avoiding the clap and possibly use the Stinkmink (near Area 6 entrance from Area 7) or other means to draw it away from watery parts you'll be able to control the fight more.

* * *

| **Even Cute Things Have Fangs** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3600 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Lagombi (Target)**<br>Great Izuchi<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Lagombi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This is like earlier encounters with the cute but fanged beast, but you may find fewer supplies to help and the long-eared beast to be faster on its feet and on its belly. Fortunately not fast than the speed of Sonic Bombs (can be crafted from Screamer Sacs, sometimes a Rare Find on sale from Rondine), since its sensitive hearing will cause it to stumble from the shock which can give a big opening to attack if it is in the middle of sliding. After you get a chance to strike, you should focus on its head if you are using blunt weapons, guns or fire, but its rear if you are using sharp attacks. Its upper half is a decent target for all four.

* * *

| **Night of the Khezu** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5040 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Khezu (Target)**<br>Aknosom<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Khezu**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While the monster may not have music, this won't be a silent night as it regularly roars to stop threats from gaining momentum even from the ceiling if it gets the chance. It may be best to draw it with the scent of a Stinkmink to somewhere open where it can't hide, but if it does end up above you, you can expect it to wind its head down to the floor to strike you which can give an opening but you should be aware of how it can also shoot an especially powerful thunder ball that explodes straight below it if not drip acid.

While on the floor, an enraged Khezu will shoot the split thunder balls twice in a row without delay which can be a great chance to take it down but it may be certain death if you get nicked by even one of them due to the stun values.

* * *

| **Dango Duty** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>2520 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Great Baggi<br>Great Izuchi<br>Khezu | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, **Baggi (Target)**, Zamite, **Izuchi (Target)** |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 20 Izuchi or Baggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While answering the Call of Dango, you need to clear the perimeter for up to 20 Baggi and Izuchi though you may end up crossing paths with their leaders on the way. You should be used to the differences in their leadership styles with the Great Baggi preferring to have allies individually land attacks while Great Izuchi even has a team attack as its mounted punisher, but should focus on isolating the target.

As you approach groups without leadership, it may be best to use ambush tactics like Flash Bombs (bring your own) or to focus on using stun to stop their counterattack. Whether you end up facing the close combat scythes, long-range spit or both... Do it for the Dango and you may see Dango Tickets in your Bonus Rewards.

* * *

| **Totally Not Cool, Kulu!** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5760 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Kulu-Ya-Ku (Target)**<br>**Kulu-Ya-Ku (Target)**<br>Aknosom | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt 2 Kulu-Ya-Ku**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

In case, you were worried about taking on two Kulu-Ya-Ku at once, be at ease. For there exists no honour among egg thieves so they will clash if they cross paths which gives you an opening as their rocks aren't in your direction. The goal isn't to slay them but to keep them out of the way for Yomogi to snatch away some eggs, so feel free to capture if need be.

It may be pot luck if the bird wyverns pull out a rock to defend themselves, but you can make calculated gambles based on how they tend to guard after pulling anything up and have a lot of recovery after the giant leap. Stay cool and you'll get two Dango Tickets as your bonus rewards besides the Bestnut becoming the Super Bestnut when you order Dango.

* * *

| **Study the Sword & Shield** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5760 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, time expires or capture target slain |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Aknosom (Target)**<br>Great Baggi<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this CAPTURE quest is to **capture an Aknosom**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

To prove your worthiness for some new Sword & Shield tech, you will have to capture an Aknosom, a bird wyvern with a flair for the dramatic that almost rivals Master Utsushi. Unlike a hunt, you can't get away with the target dying so you have to show moderation while reading its tells. Your Palico buddy will call out if it is injured enough to capture, but you can also count on the UI showing the monster's lock on having a blue icon.

The usual strategy applies where you can use Flash Bombs ( **bring your own**) to down it during mid-flight after its flip attack or other actions, but you should be vigilant to avoid wasting traps as flying monsters can retreat without running through predictable paths. On that note, the Supply Box has two EZ Shock Traps, a healthy supply of Tranqs and even Trap Tools that you can craft into traps if you are resourceful enough but you can bring additional ones so some traps can be used for the beatdown.

It is in your best interest to fight the monster over the water since that prevents the fireballs from bouncing or leaving embers that can cause Fireblight, perfect for an easier capture though you will have to be cautious when it flaps back to shoot fireballs as it may dive downwards instead of spinning around. It is a rare case where Aknosom's projectiles and melee are paired together.

It may seem strange that you only need to study the monster's motions which sometimes match a Sword and Shield, just rise up to unlock the next Ultra Co- **Switch Skill: Metsu Shoryugeki!**

* * *

| **Honing Your Hunting Horn** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>6120 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Great Izuchi (Target)**<br>**Tetranadon (Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this ENDURANCE quest is to **hunt a Great Izuchi and Tetranadon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Like the last Master Utsushi quest, you don't need to bring along a Hunting Horn to show that you understand the essence of it through surviving this endurance match whether you slay or capture the lean and not-so-lean duo. There is a lot of ground to cover even in the Shrine Ruins and there aren't any additional large monsters you can count on to help soften them up before they are both in the area.

It is all up to you, your buddies and the endemic life to sing them to an eternal slumber so you can unlock the **Switch Skill: Melodic Step** for the Hunting Horn.

* * *

| **Study the Switch Axe** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>6120 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Lagombi (Target)**<br>**Khezu (Target)**<br>Great Izuchi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/26/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Lagombi and a Khezu**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While the similarities for these two monsters may seem to end white away, both the slimy Khezu and fluffy Lagombi switch between two stances in combat whether it is leaping to a ceiling or getting on all fours. Keep this in mind to capture the essence of how the Switch Axe works and to acquire the **Switch Skill: Compressed Finishing Discharge**.

* * *

| **Learning the Light Bowgun** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Arena | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5760 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Great Baggi (Target)**<br>**Great Wroggi (Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/25/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Baggi and a Great Wroggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This hunting quest in the Arena is perfect for getting a handle on Light Bowgun hit-and-run tactics since both targets will do much of the same. All is fair as you learn the ropes, so feel free to bring additional supplies that complement the Rainbow Spiribird and EZ Farcasters like Antidotes or Energy Drinks. When you are in the arena, it isn't unusual for the monsters to be allied against you unlike in the wild but you can use the good ol' circle 'n strafe to force them to hit each other till Great Wroggi is poisoned or tired then separate it with the Fence for an easier capture or to capitalise on the moment in general.

You may end up facing some smaller monsters that get called in somehow if you don't keep the pressure on but keep your finger on the trigger and you'll earn the **Switch Skill: Elemental Reload**.

* * *

| **Donning Toxicity** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3600 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Great Wroggi (Target)**<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Great Wroggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

For this hunt, you need to slay or capture the Great Wroggi. It may not be an awe-inspiring hunt at this point even in High Rank, but you can apply flashy tactics that you've developed to deal with its groupies and work with the revolving group of wild monsters if need be. Just be sure to bring some Antidotes since the Supply Box won't have your back.

* * *

| **Lost and Found** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>2520 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>Aknosom<br>Arzuros<br>Great Wroggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) |
| Smaller monsters include: **Bullfango (Target)**, Gargwa, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Felyne, Izuchi, **Jagras (Target)**, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 20 Jagras or Bullfango**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

With twenty targets to track down, you'll find that the Jagras tend to be more reserved and regroup when one of their kin goes down while the Bullfango are brave enough to start fights with large monsters. You can take advantage of this on the hunt.

* * *

| **Blue, Round, and Cute** |
| --- |
| Rank: 4★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4680 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, time expires or capture target slain |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted: <br>**Arzuros (Target)**<br>Great Wroggi<br>Tetranadon | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this CAPTURE quest is to **capture an Arzuros**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The supply box has EZ Shock Traps, EZ Tranq Bombs (and also Tranq Ammo) besides even Trap Tools which can all help with catching the blue bear.

* * *

| **The Restless Swamp** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7200 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **Wild Monster(s) Sighted:**<br>* * *<br>**Jyuratodus (Target)**<br>Arzuros<br>Great Wroggi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/72/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/24/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Jyuratodus**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Jyuratodus, the sinister shadow of the swamp, has two stances to stalk its prey in with one being standing and the other being swimming. It very much prefers swimming, so you'll regularly find it in the muddy marshes where it has the choice and switches between both as it sees fit. It is key to note that most of its weak points (head, tail and neck - but not legs) are in reach at all times, but that it dislikes being struck by thunder attacks the most in the legs and torso.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/65/)

The Supply Box has EZ Meat Bait that can help do damage even when the monster is underwater, Nulberries for Waterblight if its mud shots make a direct hit and EZ Screamer Pods can force it to the surface while open for a counterattack when it dives and you can see the mud is bubbling. With all of this said, it may be more ideal to draw the monster out of the water by attacking from a distance or waiting out of reach as you can benefit from slopes as a movement option (or possibly Affinity Sliding) and limits its actions to only grounded attacks.

In the transition from the water, Jyuratodus can still swim through the land but when it gets grounded, it is limited to a double spinning tail attack and hip check as its primary options though it can shoot three mud shots with running in-between for a bit of mobility that doesn't help its angles of attack very much. If you allow it to get back to the water, the mere act of it swimming can leave a trail of muck that makes it harder to escape not only the shots if they hit the ground and it can also dive into the mud leaving only bubbles behind (only Giganha or Sonic Bomb equivalents can make an impact) before leaping to strike a target. Mind that it will splash a ton of muck around if it is enraged and effectively bring the swamp to you.

* * *

| **Foul Play in the Forest** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5760 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Royal Ludroth (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Royal Ludroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If there are two things that the client and the target have in common, both have their eyes on the watery turf of the Flooded Forest and both are all about **Family**. You've only got one job to focus on with handling the High Rank Royal Ludroth and may be harder to get a hit on after its experience, but the weak points are the same from the head to the mane to the tail. It is hard to count on the same monsters being around if you are hoping for help from them, so count on yourself and those you can call your own kin.

It is still able to throw down with its severable tail extending its range, but it is more cautious with shorter water shot dashes and even does its launching shot more regularly without being enraged, but you may have less trouble if you lead it to the drier part of Area 2 or even face it in Area 3 if you want to avoid its water shots from getting extended range, but still have to mind that the foam left can still cause Waterblight.

Towards the end of the hunt, you may find the previously proud pack leader trying to rest underground in Area 14 with its friends. This can be a tougher fight due to less room if you don't use a Snowbeetle (can be found on higher ground there) or the EZ Meat Bait to slow its movements. Mind that it is increasingly likely to do its swim attack (follows wriggling its head from a low profile) later into the fight which covers a long and linear amount of space quickly even on the ground.

* * *

| **Rise above the Mud** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>5760 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Barroth (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | **![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/31/)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Barroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you can rise above the monster's muck, you'll have a good chance of standing strong in High Rank. Like before, the mid-range is controlled by mud (leaves muck or inflicts Waterblight on hit) that's shaken off as it does tail and body attacks and you need a really sharp weapon if you don't want to be deflected back when you strike its head (best to target the body). You have to be especially careful when you fight the monster from maximum melee range for most weapons as it can **180° turn to land with a head smash** which is less effective if you are close enough to evade while crossing through or to the side.

Otherwise, it is a linear though powerful monster which should probably have its tail severed to limit its power and potential muck, the latter being how it keeps prey in place for its direct charge which can bounce off walls. It may be a strong strategy to fight it near cliffs (Area 2) as you can retreat off and back without using your Wirebug gauge in addition to using Flash Bombs to misdirect it, guarding or using counters while its muck is weaker. When the monster eats in Area 8, you may be able to prepare a trap.

* * *

| **Bowled Over** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8280 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br> **Volvidon (Target)**<br>**Volvidon (Target)**<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt 2 Volvidon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you don't want to be bowled over, take care when facing the crimson cannonballs since they can fire nasty paralysing spit in front even at some shallow angles (no cure besides being launched, can be blocked) in front and inflict a stench around it that makes it impossible to stomach any consumable item (bring Deodorant). It may be best to try using Endemic Life or similar alternatives to restore yourself if you run low on supplies.

The monster is still a menace from how it can barrel forward and backwards quickly, even with the ability to pivot around small hazards like rocks to make its mark but if you can land a massive impact as it rolls either through an explosive or even just Trapbugs, the monster will go careening backwards which gives one of its biggest openings to be attacked. More common openings include after it does its bouncing attack, during its stench attack and as it unfurls from its jump especially since its head ends up towards the initial target. You can also force it to lower its head while bipedal with Flash Bombs.

* * *

| **Rotten Fruit** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7200 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Bishaten (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Bishaten**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While Bishaten tends to prefer the forest to the flood, you can use EZ Meat Bait from the Supply Box (or your own supplies) to get an edge in interrupting its varied attacks and stances. Starting with its fruit attacks, one new attack has added a **shocking fruit (green)** attack where it flips forward to leave a gas that causes paralysis while it maintains its jumbo, poison and flash attacks (orange, purple and yellow respectively). Mind that this means that it has the sly beast's random fruit toss attack now includes shockfruit, but it can also throw a "fastball" Jumbofruit which is when it is standing rather than moving on its hands, feet or tail.

Its other attacks have gotten some adjustments as well as it will do claw swipes before a tail flip without being enraged and can now glide before landing to perform its spinning top attack. A lot of its movement options have wind pressure that can both help and hurt threats including the landing portion, so you'll have to plan around it to avoid getting cornered. It is important to recognise that the tail flip can be followed by a spinning strike (after balancing on its front half) when it is enraged so you don't overreach.

Like before, Bishaten is most exposed while it is balanced on its tail since it will fall to the ground if toppled by enough damage and drop random fruit which cannot be kept after the quest but can aid the hurt. Be vigilant to strike as it recovers from throwing random fruit or is balancing in general, though it can spin in place or use wind pressure to slow your assault. For a smaller payoff, you can look for its poison and shockfruit attacks when it misses since the monster covers its mouth till the gas disperses. This can be effective enough that the monster will shield its head with its forearms when tired, it can't even balance on its tail then.

* * *

| **Go Away, Pukei!** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7200 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Pukei-Pukei (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Pukei-Pukei**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

In high rank, a Pukei-Pukei is more regularly switch between their head, tail and toes tactics with its tongue, poison and flight helping to extend its reach but they are also keener to consume. For that reason, it is important to know where its next meal may be as well as the power related to what it eats. Much like how hunters can feel powerless without Dango, Pukei-Pukei can become a lot easier to deal with once it is cut off from its supply.

You won't get Antidotes in your supply box, but you can find an Antidobra at the top of the waterfall to the west and similarly count on Endemic Life like the Trapbugs (Area 1 near the giant arch, between Area 7 and 8) to interrupt Pukei-Pukei's meals if you absolutely need to stop it though a Flash Bug, Trap or massive amounts of damage can also do the job. Mind that the monster can hop and stomp in place when under pressure which can cover blind spots that its flying attack (which doesn't turn very well) cannot and you'll be able to harvest the rewards from a successful hunt.

* * *

| **Miner Problem** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7200 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Basarios (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Basarios**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you want to make Basarios sleep like a rock in High Rank, it is best to be prepared for its variety of ailments (Fireblight, Poison, Sleep) if you have a style of hunting where you can get close and personal. The weakest points (the severable tail tip, leg, abdomen, head and wing) are mainly distinct from how they don't bounce back and deflect but still work tacking on damage towards if you are far away even with the only option are kunai.

The monster may seem like it would be armoured against threats completely, but is a big softie towards all elements (water and dragon preferred) and even all ailments with the main examples being Sleep, Blastblight, Poison and Waterblight. Mind that the monster becomes more capable to chase enemies when enraged as it can walk back with a tail whip after its running chase (its rolling attack is always unsafe - but wait till it stops moving if you are closing in) and as it lobs three fireballs in quick succession. Be sure to break the plating on its abdomen and you'll be in for a gold mine.

* * *

| **Charmed by a Queen** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>7200 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Rathian (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rathian**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The master of the land, Rathian, can be difficult to down without an action plan for how to attack while it is grounded. You can focus on punishing its tail flip as it lands (two tail flips when enraged - bring your own Antidote for the poison) but it is more ideal to have Flash Bombs or Flashflies to suddenly bring Her Highness down to earth. Mind that the monster moves with more strength as its feet grace the ground, so it will be more mobile and recovers surprisingly quickly from its single strong fireball attack even as an inferno is left in its wake.

* * *

| **Grasp the Great Sword** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10080 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |

| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt 2 Bishaten**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

For this quest, you'll have to recognise the art of capitalising on openings which is best shown by Bishaten when they either paralyse or stun their prey with fruit before using their most powerful attacks with rage that could be mistaken for a Rajang. If you succeed with moderating your approach, you'll claim the **Switch Skill: Rage Slash**.

* * *

| **Honing Your Hammer** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>9720 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |

| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Pukei-Pukei and a Barroth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

As you hone your heart to be one with the Hammer, you will find that thrashing and pummelling are at the core of the art of hunting. Barroth shows this with how it hammers its head at a moment's notice while Pukei-Pukei... is there too. After clobbering the desert duo, you'll gain **Switch Skill: Water Strike**.

* * *

| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>9320 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |

| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **capture a Rathian**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

To catch the next glimpse of the Lance's potential, you need to know the lie of the land and nothing could be a better test than capture the lavishly green Queen that stands above it all. The supply box has all you need to prevail (Trap boxes, Tranq Items, Traps) so as long as you stand your ground and you should grasp the **Switch Skill: Spiral Thrust**.

* * *

| **Charge Blade Coalescing** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>9720 z |
