| Your goal in this ENDURANCE quest is to hunt a Royal Ludroth and a Jyuratodus. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

To break the boundary and understand the Charge Blade, you must bend to the will of the water whether it is clear as the sky or opaque as a rock. If you endure the waves, you will become polished and gain **Switch Skill: Axe Hopper**.

* * *

| **Hone Your Heavy Bowgun** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Arena | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>9720 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Basarios (Target)**<br>**Volvidon (Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/39/) |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Basarios and a Volvidon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Here in the arena is you can adapt the circle-strafing strategy from the ball-like Volvidon and appreciate the firepower of the boulder-like Basarios. If you make use of the towers, fence and perhaps EZ Farcaster you may reach the next plateau and earn the Switch Skill: **Counter Charger**.

* * *

| **Bad Review** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3240 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku<br>Pukei-Pukei | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/36/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, **Rhenoplos (Target)**, Altaroth, **Kestodon (Target)**, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 20 Rhenoplos or Kestodon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you want a **Five Star experience** at this Sandy Plains bash, come prepared to avoid getting thrashed. When noticed, a direct approach around Kestodon is likely to bounce back but you can **sharpen your skills or use methods that ignore sharpness** to get around the bumps. While Rhenoplos is an easier neighbour to deal with in part as they laze about, you will usually find them in far smaller numbers at once in comparison to the Kestodon and its kin. Otherwise, you can get a bigger opening if you stun the locals and shouldn't pay any mind to Kulu or Pukei.

* * *

| **Flooded Forest Fiasco** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>3240 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Great Wroggi<br>Royal Ludroth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/27/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, **Wroggi (Target)**, Gajau, **Ludroth (Target)**, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 20 Ludroth or Wroggi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

For an easier time when fixing this fiasco, you may want to bring some Meat Bait in addition to the Supply Box's EZ samples as it can help with taking down the large Ludroth in their own domain. That being said, it isn't the wrong decision if you focus on the Wroggi instead as both of them have their larger leaders around.

* * *

| **Shadows Over the Frost** |
| --- |
| Rank: 5★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8280 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Khezu (Target)**<br>**Tetranadon (Target)**<br>Lagombi | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/34/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/30/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/29/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **hunt a Khezu and a Tetranadon**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

In this High-Rank hunt, you may want to pit the two targets against each other particularly as Tetranadon has a bad time against Thunder attacks and Thunderblight.

* * *

| **A Bewitching Dance** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10800 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Mizutsune (Target)**<br>Aknosom<br>Bishaten | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/33/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Mizutsune**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Normally a serene sight to see, whether at rest or dancing in the distance, male Mizutsune in mating season are hopping mad at heart if they sense a threat in their territory. The lithe leviathan will swerve and drift across the area after it lathers up, but you can take advantage of this as it becomes more likely to fall with the right tactics. After you get the chance, its foreleg and claws are most weak to electric damage but the head, neck, dorsal fin, hind leg and tail take the most damage from sharp, blunt and gun damage. Mind that it may be fatal for any hunter if they don't prepare by bringing their own Cleansers and Nulberries for Bubbleblight and Waterblight.

Before the target boils in anger, it is worth recognising that its varied attacks have many blind spots which don't reach prey though it makes up for this through splash damage mainly from its hail of bubbles from leaps, gentle spinning and flicking its tail, not that it can't smash its tail down with the grace of a Tetsucabra. It creates a theme where its most powerful melee (tail slam, also divebomb when angry) and projectile attacks give a big opening, but it can be difficult to avoid them if you are blighted or surrounded by bubbles. Fortunately, white bubbles can be popped by kunai if need be but you shouldn't lose sight of an enraged Mizutsune as it may leave bubbles just before coiling its head above its body for a 720° spin with a water beam (big opening especially if you can get out of reach by getting too close or high). Similarly, the monster may fire a straight beam upwards before focusing straight ahead without pause, this attack is similar to another one where it shoots up before stopping and doing a 180° water beam that crosses from its side. The differences don't stop with the narrow-angle since the straight beam has a **lot of recovery** and is often done after a slide forward that leaves a lot of bubbles.

If you can take advantage of the green bubbles to heal and red bubbles to power up while recognising the patterns with each attack and the monster's state... You may be HR6 material in the making.

* * *

| **Skies Flash, Clouds Boom** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10800 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Zinogre (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/62/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Zinogre**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

It's no shocker that High Rank Zinogre hits hard, though you may find that you have to take care when pursuing the monster when it charges. This is as it often does its running tackle into charging up, but cancels it earlier to swipe at threats unless desperate. You can prevent the monster from collecting Fulgurbugs by using enough sheer force, by using a method to blind it (though it can charge while blinded) or Trapbugs which can be found in Area 1 and between Area 7 & 8 on the beaten path.

Once it reaches its charged form, it adds to its monster by being able to leave a trail of delayed thunderbolts after hopping backwards so you should be wary of the leftover bugs that serve as a hint. Still, this attack is high commitment as it only reaches a Zinogre length or so ahead of the beast and as the monster won't move til the bugs return. By comparison, its run -> skid -> three hit charged combo of stomps with its forelegs has really improved tracking so beware if its target gets far behind it somehow.

Mind that it isn't pure aggression as it has retreating attacks like a smaller tail slam before leaping back to shoot balls of electricity when in its normal form and sometimes leaps away with a spinning tail attack while supercharged. Patience is key so you find the bigger openings both from newer attacks and like its Tail Flip.

* * *

| **A Somniferous Elegy** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8640 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Somnacanth (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Somnacanth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

You may want to **bring Energy Drinks** to avoid succumbing to the Somnacanth's sedatives or to have Sonic Bombs to prevent two of its sleep dust attacks (one that disperses from the monster all around it, another which happens when swimming), but neither are the best way to bring an end to its soporific songs on land. When the siren sings, just take that as a cue to leap nearby or to get to higher ground since the opening is larger than if you use items to stop it.

It is best to draw it to the drier parts of the cold blue caverns as it will stop its backstroke attacks completely (from its old swim and singing to sleep to its shell attacks - blinding yellow, burning red and **a new green one... which heals hunters**). As for what other attacks to expect, the monster is likely to claw with its talons and attacks twice while on its front, but only once when on its tail. Its attacks from its tail standing stance usually leave it at risk, but one new inclusion has it sway its body forward (launches on hit) rather than pivot in place so that needs some consideration.

* * *

| **Sharpening Amber Fangs** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8640 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Barioth (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/53/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Barioth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you don't want to end up sharpening amber fangs, it is worth breaking the ice by severing its tail and destroying the thorns on its forelegs. This helps with reducing Barioth's mobility, but a flash bomb or trap can help with keeping the monster in place. Flash bombs are particularly useful if it falls from mid-flight or gets into a loop of backstepping while being cornered. The Ice Cyclone shot is powerful but serves as a cue to advance and get some hits whenever it is off target and fires it from the ground.

As far as the pouncing attacks, recognise that when its **forelegs are positioned under its head** that it will leap and swerve & turn to face the target but that Barioth will fall when its thorns are broken. Another tell that is important to know if that know is that when Barioth swings its tail (even when severed) that it is long enough to brush away foes from that side and also in front of its face.

* * *

| **Skies are Gray** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>8640 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Tobi-Kadachi (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tobi-Kadachi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Tobi is more of a fan of the forest than the flooded areas which give the beast an advantage as it is likely to be further away from parts where EZ Meat Bait (supply box as usual) helps hunters, large monsters may wield water attacks against it (Waterblight prevents Tobi from charging up) and also open areas where prey can evade easily. So you may want to draw the monster down to Area 6 or 7 with Stinkmink, since it will force the monster to fight outside its comfort zone.

On the note of what you can bring to weather the storm of attacks, Poison is especially effective whether through the talons of a Rathalos, Endemic Life or a weapon which inflicts the ailment. This will all make it easier to focus on hitting the tail which takes the most damage, but also serves as Tobi-Kadachi's main weapon against threats. It's\]spin attack from the air and tail flip from the ground have a lot of recovery as usual, but it also adds to its tools by pouncing on a target then trapping a poor escape option with a grounded tail flip.

* * *

| **Closer Than it Appears** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10080 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Anjanath (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Anjanath**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The big nose-boned Anjanath can be a tall order to take down, but it is fairly easy to find a Puppet Spider from outside the caverns if you search straight north from Area 1 (east of the Melynx's Gatherings) or to use a Stinkmink (Area 10 caverns) to draw Anjajath to a watery area like Area 7. The advantages from doing so include being able to roll less to get rid of Fireblight, having less magma to avoid and lastly having ledges to use for ledge exclusive attacks (helps with reaching the head and tail) .

In High Rank, Anjanath is still the biggest threat when the wings and snout is out, since it gains many attacks in this enraged state including spewing flames forward (a big opening and obvious from how it reels) and a new short range flame attack after swaying its head while standing in place. Mind that its most lethal attack that is likely to hit is its double jaw maul which catches bad Wirefalls and that it can do a more shallow bite multiple times in a row even when tired.

* * *

| **The Abyss Stares Back** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10080 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Nargacuga (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/57/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Nargacuga**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The pouncing shadow can be difficult to pin down as it is immune to Pitfall Traps (use Shock ones) and generally stays away from the waters where EZ Meat Bait can used to call Giganha, but it pays to find a way to take it away from the forest. This is as Nargacuga's weakest body parts are its head and its severable tail which are easier to reach with more space to avoid its **high rank double spinning tail attack** (big opening afterwards, but mind that it has **more reach behind the monster than in front**) and its classic tailspike projectile attack which is dangerous due to how the spikes on the ground still hurt hunters.

One more attack to mind is its **Enraged-Only Double Tail Slam** which is only does when enraged with red trails coming from its jet black body. It is easy to get into the habit of punishing the normal single slam as the slow walk before it is very distinct, but you should stay wary of how certain attacks get extended when Nargacuga is angry. Generally the hunt becomes easier when Narga's ability to use its abyssal tail whip is cut, but you can also blind then deafen the monster with Flash and Sonic Bombs to paralyse it or break the cutwings to achieve a similar effect.

* * *

| **Red Skies at Night** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10080 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br> **Rathalos (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/58/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rathalos**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Hunter's delight! Rathalos's rule over the red skies can only be countered with a plan to make them your own which is fortunately more possible than ever with Wirebug movement but more practical to achieve with Flash Bombs (bring your own) so you can get the upper hand. This may be needed more than ever as the Sky King will spend more time airborne than in low rank from shooting multiple fireballs as it leaps into the air even when not enraged to its talon kicks (which can follow a failed fireball) while tired which still inflict poison.

You can still count on a number of moves having high recovery which forces it to land like its tail swing from the air or its rapid fireballs while flapping in place. Mind that not all airborne attacks give a long time to respond since it has a **new mid-range stomp** that occurs after a single fireball or flapping high into the air suddenly (even when not angry). Be sure to recognise the situation before you ready yourself to strike its head or tail.

For greater control, you can use the Puppet Spider near the Melynx's Gatherings as you head north from Area 1 without needing to do enough silk damage or to lead two monsters to each other (Stinkmink from Area 10 also works). It is best to fight the monster in the cold blue caverns as the water doesn't allow the fire to spread and for slopes for added attack angles.

* * *

| **Do It for the Dango!** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br> **Nargacuga (Target)**<br>**Jyuratodus (Target)**<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/57/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/72/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Nargacuga and a Jyuratodus**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

If you avoid getting pinned by spikes and caked in mud, you may end up finding **three Dango Tickets** in your Quest Bonus Rewards and also have the **Magnaroar become the Magnacrisp**, it may only be if you exploit their habits. For example, Narga is best to fight in open areas for some room away from the tail, but you can get a big advantage for drawing Jyura onto dry land since it has mediocre reach from its hip check to its mud shot. That being said, remember the mud shark will seek out water if it gets the chance (good chance to use EZ Meat Bait) and also that only Shock Traps will be effective on Nargacuga if you attempt a capture.

* * *

| **Learn the Long Sword** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>14040 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Zinogre (Target)**<br>**Mizutsune (Target)**<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/62/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Zinogre and a Mizutsune**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While both flagships appear to enjoy a freeform yet refined flow of movement like a Long Sword, Zinogre and Mizutsune are fierce rivals that will thrash each other if they meet in a Turf War which is a good opportunity even without Wyvern Riding as their blights are very effective against each other. That being said, if you can't dance with bubbles like the best, you may want to bring Cleansers in addition to EZ Meat Bait if you want to prove you are a cut above the rest and earn the **Switch Skill: Spirit Reckoning Combo**.

* * *

| **Dissect the Dual Blades** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Anjanath (Target)**<br>**Rathian (Target)**<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/37/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Anjanath and a Rathian**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

At the heart of all Dual Blades mains is a balancing act between the urge to stay grounded with rapid strikes and quick movement or to rise up with leaping and ledge attacks, so it is the perfect challenge to face two winged wyverns with differing levels of flight.

They may be prone to fight each other in a Turf War (Stinkmink in Area 4), but that doesn't mean it isn't worth leading them into the ant hills of Area 10 to get your own chance to get close and personal during the spiral-like quick sand trap. If you understand the essence of ascent, you will get **Switch Skill: Tower Vault**.

* * *

| **Grasp the Gunlance** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>11520 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Barioth (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/53/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this CAPTURE quest is to **capture a Barioth**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While gunning for great things, it is important to capture how to be cool and collected in close quarters which is exactly the mindset that your target demonstrates with its proud posture and frosted fangs front and center. Like an novice hunter who can't resist using Wirefalls, it can be difficult to place Barioth's intentions at a glance but a closer look will give you a chance to get "in-its-face" for some big damage. The Supply Box has some items that are mainly for capturing the blizzard beast, but you can put some of them to use for additional hits that will aid you in getting the **Switch Skill: Guard Reload**.

* * *

| **Invoke the Insect Glaive** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>10440 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br> **Somnacanth (Target)**<br>**Aknosom (Target)**<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/23/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Somnacanth and an Aknosom**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Falling with style is the name of the game when using the Insect Glaive and this duo will put your fancy manoeuvres to the test through their wide spreading projectiles which cover land, water and sky. If you can avoid falling on your face, you may be flexible enough to earn the **Switch Skill: Diving Wyvern**.

* * *

| **Be One with the Bow** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| NPC | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Tobi-Kadachi (Target)**<br>**Tobi-Kadachi (Target)**<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/61/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt 2 Tobi-Kadachi**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Superior speed, awesome agility and precise positioning are key to make your shots count with a bow and it is no different for Tobi-Kadachi which even arch themselves from head to tail bolt-upright like a bow. Like an old world hunter flexing their might after a potion in front of seemingly impossible odds, it is key to make sure that you have the spacing to make sure that risks don't come to pass. You can refine this instinct by focusing on striking the tail at predictable points or by snatching a Poisonfrog (between Area 6 and 13 is one example) which is super effective against the targets.

If you can conduct yourself in battle with the skill to avoid the twin Tobi-Kadachi assault, you will find yourself worthy of the **Switch Skill: Absolute Power Shot**.

* * *

| **The Lava Caverns Sweep** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>4320 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>Anjanath<br>Kulu-Ya-Ku<br>Royal Ludroth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/28/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/38/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, **Uroktor (Target)**, Slagtoth, **Rachnoid (Target)** |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **slay a total of 20 Uroktor or Rachnoid**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This extermination mission takes you across the east side of the caverns where the flames do nothing to prevent the infestation of lava lizards and infernal insects. There isn't a special plan to keep in mind for either enemy type as they can be found in similar numbers all over the place, but it is worth stunning the Uroktor before they can dig away before doing their flaming uppercut or anything else. The Rachnoid are easy enough to trample due to lower mobility and much like Uroktor not having a large monster to protect it during this hunt.

* * *

| **Showdown in the Arena** |
| --- |
| Rank: 6★ | Locale: <br>Arena | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>16200 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Anjanath (Target)**<br>**Mizutsune (Target)**<br>**Zinogre (Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/52/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/56/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/62/) |
| Your goal in this ENDURANCE quest is to **hunt all target monsters**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

This trio of danger can be difficult to face at once, but a small arena comes with some perks even if there is no chance of a Turf War between the last two targets. Firstly, you can count on the friendly fire between the monsters to potentially add blights whether it tacks damage over time, softens their hide or increasing the chance of stun - which can be huge if you are low on resources of chances to inflict ailments yourself. Secondly, you can use the fence (that you can scale) to force close quarters against a tired or blighted monster.

Anjanath's weak points can be difficult to reach without the ledge to the north, but it comes full circle as it has few attacks that can reach opposites right under its feet or behind it. It may also be worth having means to counteract the blights if you are inflicted since the Supply Box has many things but no Cleanser or Nulberries.

* * *

| **Can't Kill It with Fire** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Rakna-Kadaki (Target)**<br>Basarios<br>Somnacanth | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/141/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/59/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/32/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rakna-Kadaki**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

While the quest title and Wandering Widow's pyromania may suggest that fighting with fire isn't effective, it is worth using the hazard in Area 13 to give the queen spider a magma manicure since it is a zone that it visits without provocation. It is during openings like this that you will find that despite its ghastly appearance that this phantasm of flame and thread is more tangible than most since its fragile abdomen that houses its children allows for two hits for the price of one. While hitting twice from the abdomen plus the rest of the body is exciting, hunters should be using ice or water preferably for elemental damage and consider that the head, leg (thread) and abdomen take the most damage in that order while recognising that exposed during select actions.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/138/)

Some other weaknesses lie with its Rakna babies which are valuable for its mobility which has more than a few similarities to Wirebug movement, but the mother monster's webs can be cut to make it lose balance. That being said, you have to be aware of how the smaller Rachnoids can shoot out webs to thread threats completely still and consider having a shortcut for Cleansers (in the Supply Box) to avoid being stomped or set alight by a follow-up attack for a real nasty time.

Its fire attacks don't end there as it has attacks to shoot flames directly (sweeping to the side then back), to its side as it crabwalks, from its children after they have been deployed (when enraged) which doesn't even count its ultimate attack which makes a twister of flame in two different variations. When there is a ceiling, this attack has the monster hang from above and shoot flames completely around it but this attack will occur while being docked from the ground when outdoors.

The easiest attacks to challenge tend to be the webbing ones, so it is key to recognise that when the monster's head only fires a single one forward rather than multiple that it is tired out though you may need to wait for a bigger opening for the head as the claws are rather tough to crack. The abdomen can also be a direct source of attacks when it gets smashed to the ground with multiple smashes being possible and web balls coming off it when it is thick with silk, so you should be cautious to avoid getting ensnared. If you can out-spider the spider, you will end up becoming a **HR7 Hunter**.

* * *

| **It's Gonna Get You!** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Frost Islands | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Goss Harag (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/55/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Felyne, Popo, Anteka, Gajau, Bnahabra, Baggi, Zamite, Izuchi |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Goss Harag**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The High Rank Harag doesn't waste any time in forming two ice clumps which may end up becoming two blades as before, but also include the chance of it forming an **Ice Gauntlet** to add to its biting cold fighting style. The ice appendages that it forms do serve as larger weak points that even swing behind its back at times ,but you can only count on attacking when you get a big opening as the limb reaper is surprisingly mobile when it isn't trying to pivot during an attack. Remember that its ice clumps take more damage than anything else, but that the head and forelegs (without the clumps) are next up in that order.

One beginner's trap may be more deadly in High Rank than it was before since there are many cases of combos from monsters where it is better to stay down than to Wirefall in the wrong direction or at all. The wicked Goss Harag freezes and shatters all assumptions as its double ground punch hits prey **off the ground** to punish them for assumign they can simply play dead and have invincibility frames cover for them. If you are looking for a larger opening, the ground smashes which quake the ground have much more recovery when it does it from standing than from a jump and the monster only does a running punch once before skidding (unless it is angry - look out for the one - two from the ice gauntlet).

* * *

| **Clad in Hellfire** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Magnamalo (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/35/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt Magnamalo**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Once engulfed in the flames of Magnamalo, it is best to have a plan whether to leap away with a Wiredash leaving a small trap (Wirefall doesn't do this) or whether to use Deodorant (bring your own) to erase the ailment. This can become trickier in High Rank as Magnamalo has more sweeping attacks which can leave vapour that is due to explode after a delay. Examples include a bite that launches a hunter into the air on hit which still leaves walls of vapour as it spins and a leaping spin attack which leaves vapour before it slams its tail down.

When the blades on its back/scute raise up, that's your chance to scoot away as it gets enraged and is due to go Magenta though you can get a lot of mileage if you master a way to punish its bigger attacks. This includes its classic divebomb (only when Magenta after stepping back) and Hellfire Cannon from its tail (as long as it isn't tired - steps back before winding up its tail). To slay the samurai-themed monster, you can also use **Trapbugs from Area 1** **and between Area 7 & 8** as it can force the beast to stop its charge even though the damage is mediocre. This will give you more of a chance to strike the armblades on the forelegs, head or to severe the tail's tip ( **tail still takes notable damage).**

* * *

| **A Muddy Invitation** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Flooded Forest | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br> **Almudron (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Bullfango, Bnahabra, Altaroth, Wroggi, Gajau, Ludroth, Slagtoth, Jagras |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt an Almudron**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

You should know muddy well that Almudron specialises in muck based attacks and wide strikes with its tail, but cannot be prevented from using attacks in special areas besides its ability to summon a series of mud columns which will occur earlier in fights than before (Area 1 & Area 7). If you want to break down its defences, it is best to use fire attacks or to inflict Fireblight which can be achieved with bombs but can be achieved with a **self-detonating method with a Blasttoad** (inside the small pyramid at the top - middle of the locale). As the monster is very long with its head and tail tip taking the most damage (followed by tail and abdomen), it is difficult for it to evade attacks unless it is suddenly turning or on the move underground.

This doesn't mean that you shouldn't take some care with this stick in the mud since it can leap and flick its muck and golden sludge even when it is submerged. These actions can be openings in themselves if you keep an eye out for when digs itself out of the mud with its forearms or leaps then dives into the mud. The hermit can be forced to crawl out of the mud by flash bombs as well besides EZ Meat Bait being good at stopping its movements due its large frame being easily caught, mind that Almudron can make land its swamp as well even if it can't raise pillars.

* * *

| **Subterranean Disturbances** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Diablos (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/54/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Diablos**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Water and ice attacks are most effective especially on the abdomen, head, wing and tail (not tailcase which can be severed off), but you have to be careful and mind that if you mess with the Diablos, you'll get the twisted horns in your side. Some means to make this hunt easier include leading the target into smashing the ant hills (Area 9 and 10) and getting trapped by quick sand, though Sonic Bombs (bring your own) are still invaluable when the monster digs into the ground. It is still the case that the sonic attacks only work when it isn't enraged and running on its pure instincts.

Once it has black dust hovering around its mouth, that's your sign that it's angry and that it is best to retreat in a wide circle while it runs or in a straight line if it is digging underground with you as a target. Mind that the monster does have a long range attack where it slams the earth with its tail (even when not enraged) to send a rock projectile towards threat and that it leaps quite far after digging into the dirt when enraged before doing horn swings that catch Wirefalls. You can avoid it entirely by leading off a cliff and hang with a Wirebug before returning, but may struggle to have enough time to punish that attack which is one of the bigger openings.

* * *

| **A Resounding Roar** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>12240 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Tigrex (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/60/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Tigrex**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The tenacious Tigrex can be difficult to keep still but that changes if you bring some **Tinged Meat** (sold by Rondine, can be made with Raw Meat and Parashroom) to take advantage of the environment since the beast has a habit for charging forward fangs first. The ant hills (many in Area 10, also Area 9) and other conveniently Tigrex jaw-sized structures can have the monster's maw get stuck which gives an opening. This pays off the most with ant hills due to the quick sand which left after the hill breaks, but **you can preserve the structure for another use if you interrupt the monster with enough force**.

You shouldn't get too comfortable with running for the hills however as the monster will walk around the hazard if it is too obvious or potentially leap upwards to reach its prey. That being said, you can expect its second jump in a row to have the beast stop in place for a bit and the same applies to its spin after a running charge or leap after a running charge.

* * *

| **Evil Afoot** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Lava Caverns | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>13680 z |
| Key | Conditions: HR 4 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Rajang (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/145/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Melynx, Rhenoplos, Gajau, Wroggi, Ludroth, Uroktor, Slagtoth, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Rajang**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Once awakened and enraged, the primal fury of the Rajang cannot be contained and even shows in how part of its pelt goes gold. When it does so, with its power maximum, it can leap into the air and cannonball itself down on targets but this is not even its final form. This is as it can buff up its arms to glow orange which makes them able to deflect all but the sharpest blows away but also allows it to leap into the air to smash the earth with thunderous force in its ultimate attack.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/148/)

It is easy to get lost in the spectacle of its transformations, you always need to be aware of when the monster may fire a beam of electricity (only impossible when tired - normal, super and buffed arms can do it) and where your allies are to avoid carting since it has poor tracking but happens after it raises its upper body suddenly. It can catch poorly placed turns, the start-up of wiredashes and forces the fight to circle around the beast which still has fast close up attacks which seem to cover all sides but its behind.

While, there is more to fear from its golden fighter form, don't assume that the demon isn't already destruction incarnate since it can still swing with its fists up to seven times after raising its upper body (notable recovery even in its super form), leap and shoot a thunder ball (high recovery) and generally has fairly little recovery on its melee attacks that miss (Spinning Swing, Body Slam, Double Swipe) with its lunging grab being an exception. This means that it is worth planning around the **great ape's bigger openings with care to hit the head and tail**, getting comfortable with slipping through its defences or considering getting another monster to do the dirty work with a **Stinkmink (Area 10)** or a **Puppet Spider (north of camp, up vines, east of Melynx's Gatherings)**.

Mind that traps are usually a good idea to keep threats in check, but that it can trample through **Pitfall Traps with ease when in its normal state** and **muscles through Shock Traps when its arms buff up in its golden state**. Be careful to either aim for the head or torso (foreleg would be good if not buffed) but consider striking the tail whether it is buffed up or in super mode as it can break its poise and send it back to normal form. Ice and Iceblight can bring an easy end to this world warrior.

Flash bombs tend to be especially effective against flying monsters and Rathalos is no exception, but you'll find that Magnamalo has a habit for leaping into the air and airdashing which can be brought down in a flash. These will great additional openings to strike, but you can put their rivalry against each other with the **Stinkmink (Area 10)** and **Puppet Spider (near Melynx's Gatherings, north of camp)**.

As your reward for avoiding getting seared by flames and hell flames alike, you will get **3 Dango Tickets and a High Commendation** in your bonus rewards. Yomogi will also be able to upgrade **Rosy Dango to the Triple Rose** due to the Lava Cavern access.

As usual, you can get two monsters outside the arena to lock their curled horns with each other but don't get it twisted by assuming that's enough when there's black mist from their mouths (which stops Sonic Booms from unearthing them). It is best to stop their movements with the quick sand but that needs you to run for the ant hills to lead them that direction. If you are careful and you'll avoid being stuck on their horns like Dango on a skewer with your rewards including an **Outfit Voucher, High Commendation and three Dango Tickets**. * * *

| **Serpent Goddess of Thunder** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Coral Palace | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>17280 z |
| Urgent | Conditions: HR 7 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Thunder Serpent Narwa (Target)** | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/144/) |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to slay the **Thunder Serpent Narwa**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The Lightning Lady manipulates electricity to float with magnetism which even shifts the ground below, but this can come at a cost as the grounds of the Coral Palace have some weapons that can be unearthed. You'll have to work to earn these chances since the fight is broken into linear phases rather than the monster's mood. **The first phase begins** with it shooting simple rings, balls, split lasers and having some moderate melee attacks before the calamity gets underway. One exception is when the monster floats upright with its head far from the ground which precedes it slamming its tail down which has a massive time window to counterattack and forms a series of platforms that hunters can climb to strike the **abdomen's thundersac**. This attack leaves a shockwave with a ring to avoid, but it is key to recognise chances to hit this part in particular though **Head, Wingarm (when charged) and Tail Tip (charged)** are all highlights which even glow.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/147/)

**Phase 2** of the hunt has the monster roar and produce more complex patterns of rings when upside down, at this point you will be able to find **ballistae on the floating rocks** that raise from the ground but shouldn't get too comfortable in docking yourself while shooting. This is as Narwa can still move while they are present with exceptions existing for some attacks with high recovery. **Phase 3** ups the stakes since the **one-shot** **Splitting Wyvernshot is unlocked** on the north side of the area, but there are also t **emporary but reappearing cannons and machine cannons** which can help with attacking weak points. There is a balancing act for when to use these as your target will sometimes coil and spiral while shooting vertical bolts of lightning and horizontal rings at varied heights if not doing a similar attack which shoots blue beams below.

For the finale in **Phase 4**, the monster will produce rings as it does its tackle attack across the arena which makes it more difficult to evade its vertical beam attack which leaves a bit of an opening at the end like usual. That being said, Narwa has a horizontal beam which sweeps across the arena though you can gain height or cover to avoid this. **This phase also unlocks the Dragonator** which can be used once like the Splitting Wyvernshot but you have to eyeball the angle to get a hit.

Victory for Kamura depends on an understanding of when to move and stand your ground, but you can **exploit the act of falling from great heights to great success**. One universal example is how falling hunters can tap ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/82/) to start hanging to a Wirebug without using the Wirebug Gauge when unsheathed (this is always the case when using a cannon or ballista) before evading to a direction, but there are tons of more examples since some attacks finish regardless of height but others have falling thresholds which will make them end early and far safer to use than normal. Once the queen is downed, your rank will reach HR8 with the cap being released up to HR20. You can also expect a **High Commendation** and the **Calamity Conqueror scroll**.

* * *

| **Return of the Bazelgeuse** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Sandy Plains | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>15120 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 8 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| Wild Monster(s) Sighted:<br>**Bazelgeuse (Target)**<br>Almudron<br>Tigrex | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/143/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/51/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/60/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Rhenoplos, Altaroth, Kestodon, Jaggi, Jaggia, Delex, Rachnoid |
| Your goal in this HUNTING quest is to **hunt a Bazelgeuse**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

The infamous Bomber Wyvern may have crashed your party's exploits with **all of the subtlety of a golden Rajang** before, but here you have fifty minutes to stop it before it invades yet another locale while scattering its scathing scales on the way without a care in the world. While Bazelgeuse is an easy hunt physiologically as its low head, long tail (the end is severable) and broad abdomen are all weak points for sharp, blunt and gun damage, this is only when you ignore the explosions that follow most of its movements which is only safe to dismiss once it is tired.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/146/)

During the hunt, you'll have to consider how most attacks including weak ones leave exploding scales which can even stick on walls with time to detonate being clear from how they glow gradually (they start off orange immediately when Bazel is mad). Two exceptions which never leave scales are its **Hip Check (always safe to punish)** and bite (low recovery - limited reach, but can turn), but this last move often follows its swaying its head which leaves a pattern of exploding scales. The scales are the main threat, but sometimes synergise with its other actions to catch bad Wirefalls. One highlight is when it flies low to the ground and leaves scales behind it in a straight line pattern before stomping down, since the low attitude flight's stumble or stomp's launch can provoke panic before the explosions are in consideration. That being said, you can **use these patterns of scales are an extra tell of its intent** since it sends scales backwards in a straight line before its tail slam.

While handling it on the ground comes with its own challenges like balancing where it faces to reduce the chances of it drawing out patterns of scales which will leave you open for other attacks if not just other explosions, things gets interesting when it takes to the skies since it can use flying to attack in many unique ways. First is simply flapping into the air from in place which stomps on the ground but leaves a vertical scale pattern from head to tail, second has it flap into the air and spiral before landing with a circular pattern of scales before landing, third has it graze close to the ground while going forward which can trip a hunter before a vertical pattern of scales or its stomp finishes the job. It also has an ultimate move where it flies three times far overhead dropping scales but the third case has it crash down on its prey.

One easy way to address its airborne strategy is to use **Flash Bombs** (bring your own) which can force it to fall, but may only pause it briefly and encourage it to crash into threats during its ultimate move. While it is grounded, you can actually **rely on its haphazard bombing to remove the Ant Hills of Areas 9 and 10** which grants you a sand trap to restrict its movement. As far as elements, the monster is weak to Thunder and its related blight primarily but it is best to focus on learning when to move since the timing for scales exploding changes when its state when the underside of the monster is orange. If you keep your hand steady to avoid Wirefalling into danger and a keen eye for the openings, you'll earn **two Outfit Vouchers** for your hard work.

* * *

| **The Avaricious Apex Arzuros** |
| --- |
| Rank: 7★ | Locale: <br>Shrine Ruins | Locale Info: <br>No outbreaks or upsurges | Reward Money: <br>17280 z |
| N/A | Conditions: HR 8 or higher | Failure Conditions: Reward hits 0, or time expires |
| **UNSTABLE ENVIRONMENT**<br>* * *<br>**Apex Arzuros (Target)**<br>Random<br>Random | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/142/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) | ![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/73/) |
| Smaller monsters include: Kelbi, Bullfango, Gajau, Gargwa, Izuchi, Wroggi, Jagras, Bombadgy |
| Your goal in this SLAYING quest is to **hunt a Apex Arzuros**. You have 50 mins to achieve this. |

Seen only recently during the rampage as a commander, this apex predator returns in full force so it will **break free from a puppet spider's with ease or not allow its potential prey to capture it alive**. For that reason, you'll have to fight it more honestly to get a chance to reach its head or forelegs especially with fire and thunder attacks, though it also has a weakness towards ice for its lower body which are a little tougher to most attacks minus its rear to blunt blows.

Differences with the HR Arzuros begin with its ability to perform two slower but more powerful claw swipes which raise the earth for more range. These swipes can fall at the end of its middle of its multi-swipe combo as well as a double claw clobber so you cannot get comfortable by assuming that only one type of swipe will occur with one exception. There is the **wind pressure swipe** that stops hunters at mid-range but forces the monster to collapse and sit if that attack is performed from it running on all-fours ( **look for it raising a forearm as it sprints**).

Normally, you can expect to have a larger opening when it leaps into the air and lands, but it will have the **double heavy slash swipes as an option as a quick follow-up when enraged (see glowing arms)** which demand a quick getaway or more caution as it can turn 180 degrees for the second swipe. If you are vigilant, you will find the opportunities to batter its beastclaws and find an **Outfit Voucher** in your Quest Bonus Rewards.

This Master Utsushi challenge has you face against Almudron and Zinogre, which show that monsters from A to Z can boast some explosive power if you allow them to get started. Almudron's wrecking ball can be disarmed if you attack it with enough force which is easy to time through a **Blasttoad from the small pyramid in the middle of the locale**, and it may be worth considering using this against Zinogre's charged form while focusing on attacking both of their tails. EZ Meat Bait is in the box as usual and may help with achieving and receiving a **High Commendation plus an Outfit Voucher**.

The risk comes from how Goss Harag's range is extended and how Rajang adds to its moves when golden and buffed up, but if you can fight through the ice and thunder, you'll get a sweet Quest Bonus Reward in a **High Commendation and Outfit Voucher**.

For the third of Master Utsushi's trials, you will be hunting down the ethereal Magnamalo and abyssal Nargacuga with no other distractions or invaders. The catlike duo both have blades on their forearms which can be broken to stun and force them to fall in a weakened state, so it may be worth considering this in addition to severing their tails. If you prove yourself to be up to the challenge, you may end up with **a High Commendation and two Outfit Vouchers**.

This part of the guide is **in-progress**, so the weapon move lists are in place but context (meters etc) is incoming. When this is finalised, each weapon should get it getting its own page while a general introduction remains on this page.

**All controls listed are based on the default Type 1 settings while using the Pro Controller**.

You would think that readers probably know their directions, but the tables should help to clear up misunderstandings around the icons in the game (I used Capcom's assets as a basis). Also, **Rise hides a ton of information**...

1. Some actions that are **listed in the input display when performed in the Training Area, but not mentioned anywhere else**. You either have to guess or know in advance.
2. There are **unlisted inputs/ commands for explained moves**. This can be the difference between knowing a move exists and being able to make regular use.
3. There are **unlisted actions** (usually tied to the Evade button) that are not mentioned in the game at all. One famous case is how you can zoom in when on the Ballista, but it generally means players need to make up names for techniques.
4. There are **misleading commands from the command list and/or training mode action display.** This makes it difficult to repeat results based on the information given.

The last one is while why this guide will be using familiar icons, but the use of them may not match completely with how Capcom explains things but it intends to be consistent and include some unused inputs. While we are on the topic, clicking (![Press Right Stick](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/102/)) or holding (![Hold Right Stick](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/100/)) down the Right Stick (R3 for PlayStation folks) is actually one of the most available inputs since it has a use **to adjust or quickly remove Lock On for large monsters**. Technically, they are buttons but this presentation just looks better.

Next up with have the combat button breakdown which is split into two broad groups. Blademaster and Gunner. You will need to look into your specific weapon (not yet here) if you want the fine details, but it is enough to get an idea of what you are in for. Finally, you can look at the alt text for images on this section for more hints or a reminder if you need it.

**Button Release** (![then Release Last Button](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/90/)) is a unique case in which the **button's release makes something happen**, it is often called "Negative Edge" in fighting game circles with "Positive Edge" being when the input is detected from the button is being pressed down. This icon shows up for a number of charged attacks in the Training Area, but this guide will make selective use of it to show when buttons have to be released.

To get the second Great Sword Switch Skill 3, Adamant Charged Slash, you are **supposed to upgrade or forge eight weapons** for the Great Sword then go to talk to **Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub**. It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that Kamura's Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

\[1\] - DRAW OK. You can press the command to do this action instantly even when unsheathed.

\[a\] There are three levels of charge besides uncharged attacks. Level 1 (Red), Level 2 (Yellow) and Level 3 (White) all do more damage than the last, but **the fourth and final level, the Overcharged Level, does Level 2 damage**. Air Attacks and Switch Skill 1 cannot be overcharged. Charging adds damage in general, but makes some attacks hit more times as well. Some attacks also **ignore levels of deflect** based on their charge.

\[b\] Moves that **carry the charge level from other actions to power-up**. Both the source and following attacks cannot be overcharged. Examples include how Strong Wide Slash is stronger if it takes the **charge level** from the Jumping Charged Slash. Leaping Wide Slash carries the **charge power-up** from Tackle which carries the original power-up from Charge.

To get the second Long Sword Switch Skill 1, Drawn Double Slash, you are **supposed to upgrade or forge eight weapons** for the Long Sword then go to talk to **Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub**. It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

\[1\] - DRAW OK. You can press the command to do this action instantly even when unsheathed. Step Slash needs the hunter to be moving though.

\[a\] Spirit Gauge attacks - these cost meter to perform when you have the meter to spare but actually build meter on hit if you are too low with some exceptions. Spirit Blade II and Switch Skill 2 in general require the meter for the actions to occur so they are always metered versions. Metered versions always cut through deflect with ease. with the earliest attacks in the combo

**The metered version of Foresight Slash has i-frames** which is hinted towards by a blue aura. Similarly the metered version of Iai Slash doesn't get deflected back by tough parts of a monster.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/149/)

To get the second Sword & Shield Switch Skill 1, **Drill Slash Combo**, you are **supposed to upgrade or forge eight weapons** for the Sword & Shield then go to talk to **Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub**. It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

Highlights include how the startup of the **Guard can be cancelled into Backstep, Rising Slash or Guard Slash so quickly that the Training Area display doesn't even display the "Guard"** \- effectively making them like draw attacks. With precise inputs, your offense and defence can be sped up immensely and this plays into its other strengths as well. Like how **Metsu Shoryugek** i shines when you allow it to take a hit at the start since it becomes more powerful and can use a guard point to deflect the damage/knockback. Metsu Shoryugeki can also be followed with a severing or blunt attack which has limited angles for landing but little recovery.

To get the second **Hammer Switch Skill 3, Charge Switch: Courage**, you are **supposed to upgrade or forge eight weapons** for the Hammer then go to talk to **Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub**. It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

While the Hammer may seem clumsy at a glance, it can combo into and out of a charge in many ways to the point that it can cancel some high commitment attacks, charge while sliding on slopes and manipulate the hunter's movement mid-air.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/149/)

To get the second Hunting Horn **Switch Skill 3, Bead of Resonance, you must upgrade or forge eight weapons for the Hunting Horn then go to talk to Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub.**. It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

If you need information on the types of all of the buffs, they are listed below.

|     |     |
| --- | --- |
| **Self-improvement**<br>Temporarily boosts movement speed and prevents your attacks from being deflected. | **Affinity Up**<br>Temporarily boosts affinity. |
| **Attack Up**<br>Temporarily boosts attack. | **Elemental Power Boost**<br>Temporarily boosts the weapon's element. |
| **Defense Up**<br>Temporarily boosts defense. | **Attack and Defense Up**<br>Temporarily boosts attack and defense. |
| **Attack and Affinity Up**<br>Temporarily boosts attack and increases affinity. | **Earplugs (L)**<br>Nullifies weak and strong monster roars. |
| **Knockbacks Negated**<br>Temporarily prevents knockback from enemy attacks. | **Tremors Negated**<br>Temporarily grants protection against ground tremors. |
| **Earplugs (S)**<br>Nullifies weak monster roars. | **Wind Pressure Negated**<br>Temporarily grants protection against wind pressure. |
| **Stun Negated**<br>Temporarily grants protection against being stunned. | **Health Recovery (S)**<br>Restores a sliver of health. |
| **Blight Negated**<br>Temporarily nullifies all elemental blights. | **Health Recovery (L)**<br>Restores health. |
| **Divine Protection**<br>Has a chance of temporarily decreasing damage received. | **Health Recovery (S) + Antidote**<br>Remedies poison and restores a sliver of health. |
| **Health Regeneration**<br>Gradually restores health for a period of time. | **Sharpness Loss Reduce**<br>Temporarily reduces loss of weapon sharpness. |
| **Stamina Use Reduced**<br>Temporarily reduces stamina depletion. | **Environment Damage Negated**<br>Temporarily nullifies certain forms of terrain-based damage. |
| **Stamina Recovery Up**<br>Temporarily increases stamina regeneration. | **Sonic Wave**<br>Generation a high-frequency shockwave.<br>Deals damage at a short range. |
| **Sonic Barrier**<br>Temporarily creates a barrier of sound that nullifies damage once. | **Infernal Melody**<br>Boosts attack considerably for a period of time. |

To get the **second Lance Switch Skill 3, Insta-Block, you must upgrade or forge eight weapons for the Lance then go to talk to Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub.** It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

Some highlights include how three evades can be chained in a row, but how only two are allowed to be Super Backstep Evades with two in a row being disallowed. This can make this tanky weapon able to move quickly if a guard or counter isn't suitable for the situation.

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/149/)

To get the **second Gunlance Switch Skill 1, Blast Dash, you must upgrade or forge eight weapons for the Gunlance then go to talk to Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub.** It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

Some highlights include how the Gunlance doesn't actually need to worry about menu spots being saved for ammo which leaves it in the middle of being a long range weapon and close range weapon as far as handling goes. You do need to take care of its condition since it is unable to fire Shelling rounds when it is in the red. It is worth considering Lunging Upthrust as a movement tool as it allows you to interrupt it before the strike connects to move forward and use Shelling directly ahead, to fire shells above after the move ends and automatically activates Jumping Smash if you go off a ledge with that initial dash.

The likely favourite will be Blast Dash, its Switch Skill 1 alternative though it has some tradeoffs even if it is incredible for movement across open spaces especially if you catch some air from a Great Wirebug or Gustcrab. For more small optimisations, you can look to its Long Backstep (note the extra dirt being kicked up) since you can even do them from being knocked down though you can only backstep twice with two long ones being impossible.

To get the **second Switch Axe Switch Skill 1, Axe: Forward Overhead Slash, you must upgrade or forge eight weapons for the Switch Axe.** It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

To get the **second Charge Blade Switch Skill 2, Counter Morph Slash, you must upgrade or forge eight weapons for the Switch Axe then go to talk to Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub.** It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

Be sure to use the Guard Points at the start and end of certain attacks as well as the option to loop Hop Evades with Fade Slashes (which have a guard point at the end) for maximum potential.

To get the **second Insect Glaive Switch Skill 1, Advancing Roundslash, you must upgrade or forge eight weapons for the Insect Glaive then go to talk to Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub.** It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

The Insect Glaive may not be alone or even the fastest weapon if you want to have multiple adjustments mid-air, but it excels in flexibility since the (Strong) Jumping Advancing Slash allows you to bounce into the air three times without touching the ground and as the weapon allows hunters to cling to walls for a breather. Keep in mind that you need to be on the ground to get the most of your Kinsect though, with its Harvest Extract and Recall being options on the move, with the direct Kinect: Fire needing you to be locked in place. This is important to get Strong variants of melee attaches when you get a Red Extract.

Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

Light Bowgun is about being light on your feet since most actions allow you to a Hop Evade -> Slide Evade right after and Fanning Vault even allows hunters to flank from above with the rare ability to fire ammunition and use the aerial reload in a single mid-air bound.

| Switch Skill 1 | **Melee Attack** | ![X Button](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/86/)+![A Button](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/82/)<br>ALSO AFTER **Free Silkbind Glide** |
| **Tackle** |
| **Midair Melee/Reload\*** | (MID-AIR) ![X Button](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/86/)\- reloads if not cancelled by evading or other actions<br>ALSO (MID-AIR)![ZR Button](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/89/)or![X Button](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/86/)when no ammo loaded |
| Switch Skill 2 | **Counter Shot** | ![R Button](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/85/)+![X Button](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/86/) |
| **Counter Charger** |

![](https://gamefaqs.gamespot.com/ffaq/12/149/)

To get the **second Heavy Bowgun Switch Skill 1, Tackle, you must upgrade or forge eight weapons for the Heavy Bowgun then go to talk to Master Utsushi in the Gathering Hub.** It may be best to upgrade/forge in the **Hub Prep Area** to save time when checking the Master for a **yellow** "OK, Ace! Lesson time!" prompt above his head.

Keep in mind are that you can **craft a series of five or six weapons along the Kamura, Ore and Bone Trees** while making some slightly more expensive ones as you go along to save resources and remember that the Kamura Map will not show update to show that he is available immediately after forging/upgrading - even if you reset the game.

## Controls

### Bow

> **If you find that any information in this guide is incomplete, please provide feedback!** I have plans to update each of these with more complete movesets and visual guides whenever it is practical, but advice is appreciated.
>
> My goal is to be exhaustive, but not too exhaustive and to end up being pretty and parsable in one go. Also note that not all of the formatting and style is consistent, though it should play pretty nicely with screen readers.
>
> There are a **number of actions that are marked with an asterisk (\*)** which means that I gave them a made-up name which may be subject to change if something else is more sensible. These actions are unnamed within the game's training mode display and menus, while **some other attacks have adjusted names to make the guide clearer.**
